# Uniform Cartier sections for Fano type contractions

Coordinate `EN-103-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M3-B-TO-M-0155` | `PM-066.2` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Uniform-Cartier-sections-for-Fano-type-contractions-September-25-2026` | date 2026-09-25 | derived from the openai/math preprint Uniform-Cartier-sections-for-Fano-type-contractions-September-25-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove the Cartier-divisor conjecture of Birkar and Shokurov for rational boundaries in characteristic zero and for real boundaries over ℂ. For an <i>ϵ</i>-lc Fano type contraction with positive-dimensional base and nef negative log canonical class, a Cartier divisor through any prescribed base point can be chosen with pullback log canonical threshold bounded below in terms of the dimension and <i>ϵ</i> alone.",

      "start_byte": 457807

  ],
  "tokenizer": "o200k_base"
}

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Cartier-divisor conjecture of Birkar and Shokurov for rational boundaries in characteristic zero and for real boundaries over ℂ. For an <i>ϵ</i>-lc Fano type contraction with positive-dimensional base and nef negative log canonical class, a Cartier divisor through any prescribed base point can be chosen with pullback log canonical threshold bounded below in terms of the dimension and <i>ϵ</i> alone.

- **claim-uniform-cartier-sections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $d>0$ and every real number $\epsilon>0$, there is a real number $\tau=\tau(d,\epsilon)>0$ with the following property. Let $(X,B)$ be an $\epsilon$-lc rational pair of dimension $d$, and let $f:X\to Z$ be a Fano type contraction of normal integral quasi-projective varieties with $\dim Z>0$. Suppose that $-(K_X+B)$ is nef over $Z$. For every closed point $z\in Z$, there are an open neighbourhood $U$ of $z$ and a nonzero effective Cartier divisor $D$ on $U$, with $z\in\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}D$, such that $$\bigl(f^{-1}(U),B\|_{f^{-1}(U)}+\tau f^*D\bigr) \quad\text{is log canonical.}$$
  - Explanation: For fixed dimension and discrepancy gap, a uniform positive coefficient exists so that a Cartier divisor through any base point keeps the pullback pair log canonical over a neighbourhood. The constant does not depend on Cartier indices or boundary denominators.
- **claim-birational-klt-complements** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-birational)): For each $d>0$ and $\epsilon>0$, there is a positive integer $n=n(d,\epsilon)$ such that the following holds. Let $X$ be an $\epsilon$-lc rationally Gorenstein variety of dimension $d$, and let $f:X\to Z$ be a birational Fano type contraction with $-K_X$ nef over $Z$. For every closed $z\in Z$, there is an effective rational divisor $\Delta$ over a neighbourhood of $z$ such that $(X,\Delta)$ is klt there and $n(K_X+\Delta)$ is Cartier and linearly trivial there, after shrinking the neighbourhood.
  - Explanation: Birational Fano type contractions with nef negative canonical class admit klt complements of uniformly bounded index. This is the new input to Chen's reduction.
- **claim-real-boundaries** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-real-boundaries)): Over $\mathbb C$, the conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> holds for effective real boundaries $B$ with $K_X+B$ real Cartier, with the same uniform dependence on $d$ and $\epsilon$.
  - Explanation: Over the complex numbers the Cartier section conclusion holds for real boundaries with the same uniform dependence.
- **claim-comparison-long-intervals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-a-comparison)): Let $X\to S$ be a sequence of Fano type contractions as above. Suppose $a_t$ are effective generalized CYs over $S$, continuous finite rational piecewise affine families of discrepancies (with a finite subdivision of parameter per example, so the pieces can be treated on a common resolution), on $0\le t\le L_i$ or $-L_i\le t\le L_i$ with $L_i\to\infty$. Pieces and endpoints can be taken rational. Values at each valuation are concave in $t$, and $a_0$ is klt. Assume the corresponding one of the following (one-sided for $0\le t\le L_i$; two-sided for $-L_i\le t\le L_i$):* 1. *(one-sided) the discrepancy functions are nonincreasing, and whenever $a_0(T)\to0$, $a_t(T)/a_0(T)\to1$ for every fixed $t$;* 2. *(two-sided) for some fixed $\delta>0$, whenever $a_0(T)<\delta$, $a_t(T)/a_0(T)\to1$ for every fixed signed $t$.* *As throughout these lemmas, tests on sequences of valuations include along subsequences. Then on every fixed bounded parameter range the data are eventually klt over the generic point of $S$. Write $b_t$ for their discriminant discrepancies on $S$. In case <span class="upright">(i)</span>, they are nonincreasing and satisfy $b_t(P)/b_0(P)\to1$ for every fixed $t$ whenever $b_0(P)\to0$. In case <span class="upright">(ii)</span>, some fixed $\delta'>0$ gives the same convergence for every fixed signed $t$ whenever $b_0(P)<\delta'$. These conclusions include every subsequence and primitive prime orders on every birational model of $S$.
  - Explanation: Discrepancy ratio control along long parameter intervals transfers from the total space to the discriminant on the base.
- **claim-pullback-phases** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-a-pullback-phases)): Let $a$ be a sequence of klt effective generalized CYs over $S$ in the same Fano type setting, and let $H$ be a sequence of $\mathbb Q$-Cartier $\mathbb Q$-divisors on the respective bases $S$. Suppose for some fixed positive integer $m$, for every $a(T)\to0$ the phase $v_T(m f^*H)\bmod 1$ has lifts to $\mathbb R$ of size $O(a(T))$ bounded below by $-o(a(T))$. Then with discriminant $b$, for some fixed positive multiple $m'$, $v_P(m'H)$ satisfies the same phase condition wherever $b(P)\to0$.
  - Explanation: A phase condition on the pullback of a divisor descends to the discriminant base after a fixed multiple.
- **claim-linear-chamber** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-a-linear-chamber)): In the same setting suppose $a$ is an lc effective generalized CY over $S$, with values in $(1/p)\mathbb Z$ for fixed $p$, and $a+t d$ for $0\le t\le t_0$ are effective generalized CYs, klt for positive $t$, $d\ge0$ on all divisorials; take $L_t$ affine, and $t_0>0$ fixed rational. Then eventually there is a uniform smaller $t_1>0$ on which discriminants equal $a'+t d'$ with $a'$ of bounded denominator (i.e., the same $p'$ works on all models). There is also the following version with extra data:* 1. *$a+\lambda h+s c$ are effective generalized CYs over $S$ for $0<\lambda\le\lambda_0,\ 0<s\le\lambda\theta_0$, $\lambda_0,\theta_0>0$ fixed, $h$ of bounded denominator, $c\ge0$;* 2. *on $a=0$, $d\le c\le C d$, $-\eta_i c\le h\le Cc$ with fixed $C$ and $\eta_i\to0^+$.* *Then there are uniform $\lambda_1,\theta_1>0$, with $\lambda_1\le\lambda_0$ and $\theta_1\le\theta_0$, such that eventually, on the sector $0<\lambda\le\lambda_1$, $0<s\le\lambda\theta_1$, the data are klt generically over $S$, their discriminants are exactly $a'+\lambda h'+s c'$, and $h'$ has bounded denominator. Moreover, eventually there is *no* divisorial $T$ horizontal over $S$ with $a(T)=0,\ h(T)<0$. In particular for $S$ a point exact nonnegativity on $a=0$ follows even though only the approximate bound $-\eta_i c$ was assumed.* *In this statement the minima are lexicographic: first minimize $a/e$, then $d/e$ on its minimum locus; for the extra data first minimize $a/e$, then $h/e$, then $c/e$. Here $e\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_P=T\|_{k(S)}$. Thus $a'$ itself is the discriminant in discrepancy convention even if the endpoint is not klt generically. The family $a'+t d'$, *including its endpoint at zero*, has the generalized effective log data (with nef b-part and total logarithmic divisor $L_t$).
  - Explanation: Discriminants of an affine perturbation family are affine on a uniform smaller chamber with bounded denominators.
- **claim-lc-place-tools** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-a-lc-place-tools)): Let $g:X_1\to W$ be a Mori step as above, and let $A_*$ be an effective generalized CY discrepancy on $X_1$ over $S$.* 1. *Suppose that $A_*$ is lc over the generic point of $W$, and that an effective generalized klt CY over $S$ is available for perturbation. Any prescribed horizontal divisorial lc place remains an lc place of an ordinary boundary $\Omega$, effective on the generic fibre $F$, with $n(K_F+\Omega_F)\sim0$, for a bounded positive integer $n$.* 2. *For $P$ on a birational model of $W$, suppose $A_*$ is klt generically and $T$ computes the discriminant $b_*(P)$. There is an ordinary $n$-CY over the DVR, lc in its b-discrepancies and effective horizontally on $F$, with $n$ bounded, keeping $T$ as an lc place. Here $n$-CY means that the ordinary total log canonical divisor, multiplied by $n$, is linearly trivial over the trait. Moreover for the adjusted discrepancy $A_*-b_*(P)e$, any two minimizing divisorials can be joined by a chain/interpolation of lc places in the following sense: on a log resolution over the generic DVR their strict divisors (or extractions of them) belong to a connected snc floor consisting of vertical coefficient-one divisors with monomial cones at the intersections. One can include any additional finite discrepancy data in the resolution. In particular normalized evaluations $a/e$ of such other data interpolate continuously along the chain, using rational weights to approximate intermediate values by divisorial evaluations.
  - Explanation: Tailored bounded complements preserve a chosen lc place, and minimizing places are joined through a connected locus.
- **claim-uniform-charts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-a-uniform-charts)): At a chosen vertical place in Lemma <a href="#lem:A-lc-place-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:A-lc-place-tools">4</a><span class="upright">(ii)</span>, after a bounded degree extension of $k(W)$ and a chosen extension of the DVR and of the place, there is a toroidal cone representing the place, with nonnegative coordinates $$\label{eq:A-contact-cone} (x,e_*),\qquad M x=r e_* .$$ Here $M$ ranges over a finite list of integral matrices of bounded sizes, $r$ is integral (possibly huge), $e_*$ is value of the new uniformizer, and original uniformizer value is $\rho e_*$ with positive bounded $\rho$. Nonzero integral solutions on the cone give divisorial valuations (not necessarily primitive after restriction); evaluations of discrepancy data always refer to homogeneous values by restriction. For horizontal places as in the first bullet one just has an orthant on a bounded generic resolution, without $e_*$. For *any* effective generalized CY data $A$ on $X_1$ over $S$, values on these cones are finite continuous homogeneous piecewise linear, and are Lipschitz in $x$ with uniform constant *at fixed* $e_*>0$ (uniform homogeneous Lipschitz in the horizontal case). Constants depend on the boundedness in this Mori step and the complement indices, *not* on $r$, vertical coefficients, or the nef part index or the chosen data $A$. In particular finitely many differences at fixed parameter separations have uniform Lipschitz bounds as well.
  - Explanation: Toroidal cone charts give discrepancy functions that are piecewise linear with uniform Lipschitz bounds independent of the contact and nef-part index.
- **claim-toroidal-charts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-a-toroidal-charts)): For the prescribed place and tailored complement in Proposition <a href="#prop:A-uniform-charts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:A-uniform-charts">5</a>, there are charts with its stated contact equations and integral-valuation property. The prescribed place is monomial on one of these charts. They have rational functions $z_1,\ldots,z_f$, $f=\dim F$, with uniformly bounded pole degrees on a bounded embedding of $F$, such that:* 1. *their logarithmic differentials form a relative log basis in the vertical case and an absolute log basis over the generic field in the horizontal case;* 2. *their orders are linear functions of the chart coordinates with uniformly bounded slopes;* 3. *every rational monomial valuation in the vertical chart with $e_*>0$ restricts to a Gauss valuation on $K_1(z_1,\ldots,z_f)$, where $K_1/k(W)$ is the bounded extension used in constructing the chart. Horizontally the same assertion holds with the trivial valuation on the generic field.* *Here Gauss means that evaluating a polynomial in the $z_j$ gives the minimum of its coefficient-adjusted monomial weights, without cancellation among terms of equal weight.
  - Explanation: Bounded charts carry coordinate functions of bounded pole degree on which monomial valuations restrict to Gauss valuations.
- **claim-function-slopes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-a-function-slopes)): On a chart of Lemma <a href="#lem:A-toroidal-charts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:A-toroidal-charts">6</a>, the order of a nonzero rational function is continuous, homogeneous and finite piecewise linear, including on the faces. If its pole divisor on the embedded generic fibre has degree at most $m$, where $m\ge1$, its Lipschitz constant in $x$ at fixed $e_*>0$ is at most $Cm$. Horizontally this bound holds on the entire cone. The constant $C$ depends only on the fixed families and chart choices. A function with pole degree less than one is constant on the generic fibre and has zero angular variation.
  - Explanation: Orders of rational functions on charts are piecewise linear with Lipschitz constant controlled by pole degree.
- **claim-horizontal-variants** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-a-horizontal-variants)): The chart and metric conclusions have the following two variants.* 1. *The same tailorable bounded complement and homogeneous chart argument works if we are working over an algebraically closed generic field, with the relevant effective CYs on a $\mathbb Q$-factorial model of Fano type, and have boundedness only up to isomorphism in codimension one of these models.* 2. *A Cartier rational b-order function $N$ with effective trace of degree $O(\xi)$ on the bounded model, b-linearly $\mathbb Q$-equivalent to a difference of b-nef parts both of trace degrees $O(\xi)$, has horizontal Lipschitz constant $O(\xi)$, including when $\xi\to0^+$.
  - Explanation: The chart and metric conclusions persist in two horizontal variants.
- **claim-lattice-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-a-lattice-approximation)): 1. *Let $u_i$ lie in a fixed nonnegative orthant and $\lvert u_i\rvert\to\infty$. After passing to a subsequence there are $s_i\to0^+$, nonzero nonnegative integral vectors $n_i$, and a fixed, possibly subsequence-dependent, $C_*$ such that $$\label{eq:A-horizontal-approximation} \lvert n_i-s_i u_i\rvert\le C_*s_i, \qquad s_i\lvert u_i\rvert\longrightarrow\infty .$$* 2. *Let $M_i x_i=r_i$, where $x_i\ge0$, $r_i$ is integral and $M_i$ belongs to a fixed finite list of integral matrices. If $\alpha_i>0$ tends to zero, then after passing to a subsequence there are positive integers $E_i$, nonnegative integral $n_i$, and a fixed $C_*$ such that $$\label{eq:A-vertical-approximation} M_i n_i=r_i E_i,\qquad s_i=E_i\alpha_i\longrightarrow0^+,\qquad \lvert n_i-E_i x_i\rvert\le C_*s_i .$$* 3. *For every sufficiently small fixed $\gamma>0$, the same matching equations admit $n_i\ge0$ integral and $1\le E_i\le J(\gamma)$ with $$\label{eq:A-bounded-dirichlet} \lvert n_i-E_i x_i\rvert\le\gamma ,$$ where $J(\gamma)$ is uniform in $i$ and in the contact vectors $r_i$.
  - Explanation: Real chart points are approximated by integral ones with errors controlled by a small scale.
- **claim-quantized-cells** (theorem, [source](#stmt-claim-a-quantized-cells)): The bounded-denominator functions $a,h$, when present, have only a bounded finite set of possible slopes in a horizontal chart, or in integral kernel coordinates on a vertical slice $e_*=1$. Their vertical affine constant terms belong to a rational grid with bounded denominator, although their magnitudes need not be bounded. Their common cells are cut out by a fixed finite set of possible normal vectors, with offsets of bounded denominator. They are pointed, their vertices have bounded denominator, and they satisfy a uniform polyhedral distance bound.
  - Explanation: Bounded-denominator functions have finitely many slopes and polyhedral comparison cells with bounded-denominator vertices.
- **claim-phase-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-b-phase-nonvanishing)): Let $(V_i,H_i)$ be a sequence in bounded dimension, where $V_i$ is projective of Fano type and $H_i$ is a nef and big $\mathbb Q$-Cartier rational Weil divisor. Suppose that, for rational $t\in[0,L_i]$, where $L_i\to\infty$, there are generalized data with discrepancies $a_i(t)$ such that $$\label{eq:B-family} B_i(t)\ge R(H_i),\qquad K_{V_i}+B_i(t)+\mathbf M_i(t)_{V_i}\sim_{\mathbb Q}tH_i, \qquad \mathbf M_i(t)\text{ is b-nef and $\mathbb Q$-Cartier}.$$ Assume that $a_i(0)$ is klt and that $a_i(t)$ is nondecreasing in $t$ at every divisorial valuation.* *Assume the following condition, also after restriction to any subsequence. For every choice of rational $t_i\to\infty$ in the parameter range, one can pass to a further subsequence and choose a fixed positive integer $q$ such that, for every sequence of primitive divisorial valuations $T_i$ with $a_i(t_i,T_i)\to0$, the signed phases $\theta_i(T_i)$ of $qH_i(T_i)$ satisfy $$\label{eq:B-phase-hypothesis} -o\bigl(a_i(t_i,T_i)\bigr) \le \theta_i(T_i) \le O\bigl(a_i(t_i,T_i)\bigr).$$ Then there is an integer $M>0$ such that for every $i$ some $m_i\in\{1,\ldots,M\}$ satisfies $\|\lfloor m_iH_i\rfloor\|\ne\varnothing$. Equivalently, after replacing $M$ by a common multiple, the same positive degree works for all $i$.
  - Explanation: Under signed phase control a bounded multiple of the polarization has a section.
- **claim-seifert-bundle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-b-seifert)): Let $V$ be projective normal, and let $H$ be a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier rational divisor. There is a projective normal compactified Seifert bundle $p:Y\to V$ with disjoint $\mathbb Q$-Cartier zero and infinity sections $E_0,D$ and a rational fibre coordinate $z$ such that $$\label{eq:B-bundle-identities} \mathop{\mathrm{div}}(z)=p^*H+E_0-D, \qquad K_Y+E_0+D=p^*\bigl(K_V+R(H)\bigr).$$ The second equality uses compatible canonical divisors. The divisor $D$ is relatively ample; if $H$ is nef, then $D$ is nef, and if $H$ is nef and big, then $D+p^*H$ is nef and big.* *In these identities, pullback of a rational Weil divisor along the equidimensional map $p$ is defined at the generic points of its prime components, using the multiplicities of their inverse images. This agrees with Cartier pullback whenever the divisor is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Orders of $H$, $E_0$, and $D$ on higher models use their Cartier b-pullbacks.* *Suppose $B\ge R(H)$ and $\mathbf M$ is a nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier b-divisor on $V$. Give $Y$ boundary $p^*(B-R(H))$ and nef part $p^*\mathbf M$. Write $a$ for the generalized discrepancy on $V$ and $A$ for that on $Y$. If a primitive divisorial valuation $v$ of $k(Y)$ restricts nontrivially to $w$ on $k(V)$, then $$\label{eq:B-bundle-discrepancy} A(v)=a(w)+\|h(v)\|+\operatorname{def}(v), \qquad h(v)=v(E_0)-v(D)\equiv-w(H)\pmod\mathbb Z,$$ where $\operatorname{def}(v)$ is a nonnegative integer. It vanishes precisely for the Gauss, or fibre-torus-invariant, valuations. If $w=0$, the primitive valuations are the ordinary places of the generic $\mathbb P^1$, and have discrepancy $1$. Consequently klt base data give klt data on $Y$ without the two sections; adding both sections gives lc data whose non-klt locus is contained in the sections.
  - Explanation: A compactified Seifert bundle exists with explicit canonical identity and discrepancy formula.
- **claim-denominator-clearing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-b-denominator-clearing)): In bounded dimension, suppose $V$ is projective of Fano type and carries effective generalized klt Calabi–Yau data with $B\ge R(H)$. After multiplying $H$ by a bounded positive integer, there is an ordinary klt log Fano boundary $\Theta\ge R(H)$. For this scaled divisor the Seifert bundle is of Fano type. The assertion does not require a bound on the Cartier index of the nef b-divisor.
  - Explanation: After a bounded scaling an ordinary klt log Fano boundary dominates the ramification boundary.
- **claim-unbounded-index** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-b-unbounded-index)): Let $F_i$ be projective of positive bounded dimension and let $G_i$ be ample $\mathbb Q$-Cartier rational divisors such that $(F_i,R(G_i))$ is klt and $$-(K_{F_i}+R(G_i))\sim_{\mathbb Q}I_iG_i, \qquad I_i>0,\quad I_i\longrightarrow\infty.$$ Let $C_i$ be the compactified Seifert cone obtained by contracting the zero section, and denote its infinity divisor by $D_i$. Then $C_i$ is klt of Fano type, $D_i$ is ample, and $-K_{C_i}\sim_{\mathbb Q}(I_i+1)D_i$. It is impossible that $$\label{eq:B-cone-small-height} v_i(D_i)=o\bigl(A_{C_i}(v_i)\bigr)$$ for every sequence of primitive divisorial valuations with $A_{C_i}(v_i)\to0$.
  - Explanation: On a cone with unbounded index the height at infinity cannot be negligible against small discrepancies.
- **claim-principal-order** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-b-principal-order)): Let $F_i$ be projective of Fano type in bounded dimension, and let $P_i\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$ be $\mathbb Q$-Cartier rational divisors. Suppose that $F_i$ carries effective generalized klt Calabi–Yau data with discrepancies $a_i$ and boundary at least $R(P_i)$. Suppose, after a fixed integral scaling if necessary, that the signed phases of $P_i$ at every sequence with $a_i(T_i)\to0$ lie in $[-o(a_i(T_i)),O(a_i(T_i))]$. Then the least positive integer $q_i$ for which $q_iP_i$ is principal is bounded.* *More generally, the same cyclic-cover argument applies whenever the induced isotrivial fibration below has $u=o(A)$ at its small-discrepancy divisors over zero. This formulation permits the argument to be used after an independent estimate of the height $u$.
  - Explanation: The principalization order of a numerically trivial divisor is bounded under phase hypotheses.
- **claim-cutoff-claims** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-c-cutoff-claims)): Suppose $\dim W_\beta>0$. After passage to a subsequence there is a fixed $\delta>0$ and a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial Fano type extraction $\widehat W\to W_\beta$ such that $\widehat W$ has ordinary $\delta$-lc singularities and every positive coefficient of the pullback of $D_W/\beta$ is at least $\delta$. A bounded constant $C$ can be chosen so that $S(CD_W/\beta)$ generates $k(W_\beta)$.* *There are algebraically independent functions $\phi_1,\ldots,\phi_{\dim W_\beta}$ in this space and a b-nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier order function $H$ on $k(W_\beta)$, pulled back to $k(V)$, with uniformly bounded denominator of its values on primitive orders, such that:* 1. *If $a(T)=0$, then $H(T)\leq CD(T)/\beta$. If $T$ is nontrivial on $K_\beta$ and all $T(\phi_j)\geq0$, then $H(T)\leq-1$.* 2. *If $D(Q)=0$ and $a(Q)\leq1$, then $H(Q)\leq0$.* 3. *If $a(T)=0$ and <a href="#eq:C-alpha" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:C-alpha">[eq:C-alpha]</a> holds at $T$, then $H(T)\geq-C\eta D(T)/\beta$.* *In particular, if <a href="#eq:C-alpha" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:C-alpha">[eq:C-alpha]</a> holds on all of $I_\beta$ with $\eta_i\to0$, those primes are eventually horizontal over $K_\beta$; that is, their restrictions to $K_\beta$ are trivial.
  - Explanation: Uniform cutoff models and a b-nef order function control floor primes.
- **claim-scaled-phase** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-c-scaled-phase)): Assume <a href="#eq:C-alpha" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:C-alpha">[eq:C-alpha]</a> with $\eta_i\to0$ whenever $a(T)=0$ and $LD(T)\to0$, along arbitrary subsequences. Let $\ell$ be a rational principal order. Suppose that for every nonfloor prime $Q$ on $V$ there is an integer $p_Q$ with $$\label{eq:C-nonfloor-phase} \|p_Q-\ell(Q)\|\leq G(Q),\qquad G\geq0,\qquad G\sim_\mathbb Qc_gLD,$$ where $c_g\geq0$ is rational and uniformly bounded. There is a fixed positive integer $q$ such that $$\label{eq:C-scaled-phase} \operatorname{dist}(q\ell(T),\mathbb Z)=O(LD(T)) \quad\text{if }a(T)=0\text{ and }LD(T)\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Rational principal orders are close to integers at floor primes of small polarization order after a fixed multiple.
- **claim-saturation-count** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-c-saturation-count)): Let $f_1,\ldots,f_s$ be a finite list of genuine rational functions on $V$, whose length may vary with the sequence, and let $$\label{eq:C-neutral-polarization} J(T)=\max\{0,\max_j(-T(f_j))\},\qquad 0\leq J\leq C L_0\mathbf D,\qquad1\leq L_0=O(L).$$ Thus $J$ is an integral basepoint-free Cartier b-divisor on a high model. Suppose <a href="#eq:C-alpha" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:C-alpha">[eq:C-alpha]</a> holds uniformly with $\eta_i\to0$ on $$\mathcal L=\{T:a(T)=0,\ J(T)=0\}.$$ In addition, on every subsequence on which $L_0\to\infty$, assume that all valuations in $\mathcal L$ eventually restrict trivially to the *field generated by* $S(L_0D)$.* *After passage to a subsequence, there are cutoff fields $K_*$ of fixed transcendence degree $p$, a fixed constant $B$, and positive real numbers $v_i$ with the following properties.* 1. *For every fixed $C_1>0$, eventually $S(C_1LD)\subset K_*$.* 2. *Eventually $S(BLD)$ generates $K_*$. If $T\in\mathcal L$ is nonnegative on this space, its restriction to $K_*$ is trivial.* 3. *There is $C'>0$, independent of the positive integer $n$, such that for every fixed $n$, eventually $$\label{eq:C-uniform-counts} \dim S(nLD)\leq C'v_i n^p,\qquad \dim\operatorname{Span}\{g_1\cdots g_n:g_j\in S(BLD)\} \geq v_i n^p.$$* *The eventual index in <span class="upright">(i)</span> may depend on $C_1$, and that in <span class="upright">(iii)</span> may depend on $n$; $B$ and $C'$ are fixed before $n$. All assertions concern divisorial evaluations. Exact triviality also holds on rational monomial subcones cut out by the indicated conditions.
  - Explanation: Bounded-cost functions lie in one cutoff field, tests are trivial on it, and section counts have matching upper and lower bounds.
- **claim-orbifold-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-c-orbifold-vanishing)): Let $\mathcal X$ be a smooth proper Deligne–Mumford stack over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, with trivial generic stabilizer and projective coarse space. Let $\Theta$ be an effective rational divisor with simple normal crossing support and coefficients less than one. If $D$ is an integral Cartier divisor and $D-(K_{\mathcal X}+\Theta)$ is nef and big, then $H^q(\mathcal X,\mathcal O_{\mathcal X}(D))=0$ for $q>0$.
  - Explanation: Higher cohomology vanishes on a smooth orbifold under a nef and big condition.
- **claim-adjoint-lattice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-c-adjoint-lattice)): Let $X$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial variety of Fano type of dimension $p>0$, and let $N$ be a nef and big integral Weil divisor. Suppose that $H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(N))$ defines a birational map. Choose an effective rational boundary $\Delta$ with $(X,\Delta)$ klt and $K_X+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$. At a general smooth point outside the supports of $N$ and $\Delta$, fix regular parameters and take total-degree-then-lexicographic Taylor initial exponents, using the compatible local trivializations of the powers of $N$. Let $$K=\overline{\operatorname{conv}} \left\{\frac{\nu(s)}j: j\in\mathbb Z_{>0},\quad 0\ne s\in H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(jN))\right\}.$$ If $t$ is a positive integer and $\alpha\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^p$ satisfies $\alpha+\mathbf1\in\operatorname{int}(tK)$, then some section of $tN$ has initial exponent $\alpha$.
  - Explanation: Interior lattice points of the jet body are realized as Taylor initial exponents of sections.
- **claim-jet-count** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-c-jet-count)): In the setting of Lemma <a href="#lem:C-adjoint-lattice" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:C-adjoint-lattice">20</a>, $K$ is bounded and contains the standard simplex $\Sigma=\operatorname{conv}(0,e_1,\ldots,e_p)$. For every integer $n\ge1$, $$h^0(X,nN)\le(n+p)^p\operatorname{vol}(K).$$ Fix any integer $t>1+2p$. The space spanned by products of $n$ sections of $tN$ has dimension at least $n^p\operatorname{vol}(K)$.
  - Explanation: Section counts are bounded above and below by the volume of the jet body.
- **claim-neutral-field** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-c-neutral-field)): In Lemma <a href="#lem:C-saturation-count" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:C-saturation-count">18</a>, let $g_{i,1},\ldots,g_{i,r_i}$ be any finite list contained in $S(C_1L_iD_i)$ for one fixed $C_1$. For all sufficiently large $i$, the entire field $F_i=k(g_{i,1},\ldots,g_{i,r_i})$, and its relative algebraic closure inside $k(V_i)$, lie in $K_{*,i}$. Thus every test from part <span class="upright">(ii)</span> is exactly zero on every nonzero element of these fields at that same index, without a degree-dependent eventual qualification. More generally, a valuation trivial on a subfield remains trivial on any algebraic extension of it inside its valued overfield.
  - Explanation: Fields generated by bounded-cost functions and their algebraic closures lie in the cutoff field.
- **claim-cone-dictionary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-d-cone-dictionary)): In the above setting the following statements hold.* 1. *The discrepancy of $(X,H)$ at $(P,y)$ is $a(P)$, and its discrepancy at a pure weight is zero. Thus $a(P)$ is a nonnegative integer for every primitive $P$, and $\mathbf u\in\operatorname{int}C$.* 2. *For rational $\mu\in\operatorname{int}C$, the ray quotient $V_\mu$ is normal and projective, with function field $\mathcal K$. The order function $$\label{eq:D-polarization} \mathbf L(\mu)(P)=\min_{y\in\Delta_P}\langle y,\mu\rangle$$ is the b-pullback of an ample rational divisor on $V_\mu$. Consequently $$\label{eq:D-height-divisor} \mathbf D(P)=\min_{y\in\Delta_P}(P,y)(h) =\gamma(P)+\mathbf L(\mathbf u)(P)$$ is the b-pullback of an effective ample rational divisor $D$ on $V_{\mathbf u}$.* 3. *At every actual prime $P$ of $V_\mu$, a minimizer $y$ of <a href="#eq:D-polarization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:D-polarization">[eq:D-polarization]</a> can be chosen so that $e(P,y)$ is a primitive prime order on $X$, for some positive integer $e$. For this choice, $$\label{eq:D-actual-prime} a(P)+(P,y)(h)=\frac1e, \qquad e y\in\operatorname{Hom}(M,\mathbb Z).$$ On each $V_\mu$, $a$ is the discrepancy function of an effective ordinary lc Calabi–Yau pair, and $a+\mathbf D$ is that of an effective generalized klt Calabi–Yau pair with nef part $\mathbf D$. Each $V_\mu$ is of Fano type. At an actual prime outside the floor $\{a=0\}$, the same minimizer satisfies $$\label{eq:D-off-floor} a(P)=1,\qquad e=1,\qquad (P,y)(h)=0.$$
  - Explanation: Affine cone data translate to discrepancy and polarization data on ray quotients.
- **claim-lc-tests-gauss** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-d-lc-tests)): Every nontrivial divisorial valuation nonnegative on $R$ and having zero discrepancy for $(X,H)$ is Gauss. Its restriction to $\mathcal K$ is either $eP$ with $a(P)=0$, or trivial, in which case it is a nonzero pure weight in $\sigma$. Conversely every rational Gauss extension with $a(P)=0$, and every nonzero rational pure weight, has zero discrepancy and a center whose closure contains the contracting vertex.* *If $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\breve R\to X$ is quasi-étale and the pairs are related by log pullback, every divisorial $h$-lc test upstairs restricts to one of these tests, up to scaling. Conversely all the rational tests just listed lift to divisorial $h$-lc tests upstairs whose center closures contain the upstairs vertex. These statements suffice in particular for testing invariant rational functions.
  - Explanation: All zero-discrepancy divisorial tests are Gauss valuations or pure weights.
- **claim-section-symbols** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-d-section-symbols)): For every positive integer $q$ and every $g\in\mathcal K$, $$\label{eq:D-symbol-equivalence} g\in S(q\mathbf J_m) \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad g\xi^q h^{qm}\in\breve R.$$ In particular the neutral functions of cost $b\in\mathbb N$ are precisely $$\label{eq:D-neutral-functions} \{g\in\mathcal K:g h^b\in\breve R\}=S(b\mathbf D).$$
  - Explanation: Sections of multiples of the section b-divisor correspond to regular symbols.
- **claim-sandwich-regularization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-d-sandwich)): Let $L\ge1$ vary with the sequence. Choose integers $m>m_0$ with $m-m_0\ge cL$ for a fixed $c>0$, and suppose both $k+m_0\mathbf u$ and $k+m\mathbf u$ are interior. Assume that whenever primitive divisorial valuations $P$ satisfy $a(P)=0$ and $L\mathbf D(P)\to0$, there are integers $j_P$ such that $$\label{eq:D-sandwich-hypothesis} j_P\le\mathbf J_{m_0}(P)\le\mathbf J_m(P) \le j_P+O(L\mathbf D(P)).$$ Then there is a bounded positive integer $q$ and a nonzero $g\in S(q\mathbf J_m)$. Equivalently, $g\xi^qh^{qm}$ is regular on $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\breve R$.
  - Explanation: An integer sandwich of section b-divisors within a small error yields a nonzero regular symbol of bounded multiple.
- **claim-character-regularization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-d-regularization)): Suppose that the order condition <a href="#eq:C-alpha" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:C-alpha">[eq:C-alpha]</a> on $V_{\mathbf u}$, with error $\eta_i\to0$, holds whenever $a(P)=0$ and $L\mathbf D(P)\to0$. Fix $C_0>0$. If $k\in M$ satisfies $$\label{eq:D-width} \|\langle y,k\rangle\|\le C_0L\langle y,\mathbf u\rangle \qquad(y\in\sigma),$$ there are a bounded positive integer $q$, a nonzero invariant rational eigenfunction $Y=fz^{qk}$, and a positive integer $b\le C_2L$ such that $h^bY\in\breve R$. The constants are uniform in $k$. Both signs of $k$ may be treated with the same bounded multiplier, and their costs may be increased to a common cost $O(L)$.* *The same bounds hold uniformly if $L$ is replaced by any $L'\ge L$ and <a href="#eq:D-width" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:D-width">[eq:D-width]</a> is imposed at scale $L'$.
  - Explanation: Characters of bounded width are regularized by a bounded multiplier at cost linear in the scale.
- **claim-character-count** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-d-character-count)): Assume the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:C-saturation-count" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:C-saturation-count">18</a> on $V_{\mathbf u}$, and use its notation $p$ and $v_i$. For a fixed $C_0>0$, let $W_{t,k}$ be the space of regular symbols in $\breve R$ of finite-group character $\chi^t$ and torus weight $t\mathbf u+k$. For every fixed positive integer $n$, eventually $$\label{eq:D-character-bound} \dim_k W_{t,k}\le C_3 v_i n^p$$ simultaneously for all integers $0\le t\le C_0nL$ and all weights satisfying $$\|\langle y,k\rangle\|\le C_0nL\langle y,\mathbf u\rangle \qquad(y\in\sigma).$$ The constant $C_3$ is independent of $n,t,k$.
  - Explanation: Spaces of symbols of one character and weight have polynomially bounded dimension.
- **claim-tilted-characters** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-d-tilted-characters)): Suppose there are boundedly many invariant rational eigenfunctions $Y_j^\pm$ of weights $\pm k_j$, with $$h^{m_j}Y_j^\pm\in\breve R,\qquad 0<m_j\le A L_j,\qquad 1\le L_j\le L,$$ where $A$ is fixed. Fix bounded real numbers $u_j$ and $\delta>0$. Assume that, at every nonzero pure weight and at all Gauss extensions over primitive $P$ with $a(P)=0$, $L\mathbf D(P)\to0$, the evaluations satisfy $$\label{eq:D-tilt-hypothesis} y_j=v(Y_j^+)=-v(Y_j^-),\qquad \sum_j\frac{u_jy_j}{L_j}\le\delta v(h).$$ It suffices to check these inequalities on rational vectors. For fixed $M_0$ sufficiently large compared with $1/\delta$, choose integers $$r_j=M_0u_jL/L_j+O(1),$$ with uniformly bounded rounding errors. Then there is a regular nonzero symbol of finite-group character $\chi^{qm}$ and torus weight $$\label{eq:D-tilt-weight} q\left(m\mathbf u-\sum_jr_jk_j\right), \qquad 0<m\le C_6M_0\delta L,$$ for a bounded positive integer $q$. The constant $C_6$ depends only on the fixed bounds for the number of functions, their costs, the $u_j$, and rounding errors; it is independent of $\delta$ and $M_0$. The bound for $q$ may depend on the fixed choices $\delta,M_0$. No order condition <a href="#eq:C-alpha" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:C-alpha">[eq:C-alpha]</a> is assumed here.
  - Explanation: A tilted height condition yields a regular symbol of controlled character, weight and cost.
- **claim-log-jet** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-e-log-jet)): Fix finitely many regular affine functions on $S$. On a fixed resolution $P$, with $F$ reduced snc, there is a constant $C$ such that, for every polynomial $g\ne0$ of degree at most $D$ in those functions and every quasi-monomial $v$ over $x$, $$\label{eq:E-jet} r_F(v)(g)\le v(g)\le r_F(v)(g)+C(D+1)A_{P,F}(v).$$ The same constant works when the centre is not closed and when some weights disappear on passing to a face of the monomial cone.
  - Explanation: Values of polynomials differ from their monomial retraction by an error proportional to degree and log discrepancy.
- **claim-slice-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-e-compactness)): The slice $\mathcal Q$ is a compact finite polyhedral complex of monomial valuations on any log resolution adapted to $h$ and the $F_j$. Evaluations of fixed functions are continuous on it. For centred valuations with $A(v)=1$, there are constants $c,C>0$ depending on the germ such that $$\label{eq:E-centering} \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(v)\ge c,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(v)\ge \frac{c}{v(\mathfrak m)}.$$ Volume is continuous on the centred part of $\mathcal Q$.
  - Explanation: The common slice is a compact polyhedral complex on which volume is continuous and bounded below.
- **claim-simultaneous-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-e-all-functions)): In the situation of <a href="#eq:E-retraction-errors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:E-retraction-errors">[eq:E-retraction-errors]</a>, suppose $A_S(v)$ and $A_S(r)$ are bounded above and $v(\mathfrak m),r(\mathfrak m)\ge c_0>0$. Then a single constant $C$ works for every such $v$ and every $f\in R$: $$\label{eq:E-all-functions} r(f)\le v(f)\le(1+Ce)r(f).$$ In particular, $\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(v)^{1/n}\ge(1+Ce)^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(r)^{1/n}$.
  - Explanation: A single constant compares a valuation with its retraction on all regular functions.
- **claim-convex-domination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-e-convexity)): For $v_1,\ldots,v_k\in\mathcal Q$ and $p_s\ge0$ with $\sum_s p_s=1$, there is $u\in\mathcal Q$ such that $$\label{eq:E-convexity} u(f)\ge\sum_s p_s v_s(f)\qquad(f\in R).$$ If a positive-weight term is centred, then $u$ is centred. On the cone of common lc rays, the same statement holds with $A(u)=\sum_s p_sA(v_s)$, without requiring $A(v_s)=1$. For an allowed height, $b(u)\le\sum_s p_sb(v_s)$.
  - Explanation: Convex combinations of slice valuations are dominated by a slice valuation.
- **claim-opposite-ratios** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-e-opposite-ratios)): Suppose rational functions $q,q'$ have regular numerators and power-of-$h$ denominators, and $v(q')=-v(q)$ on $\mathcal Q$. The interpolant in Lemma <a href="#lem:E-convexity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:E-convexity">33</a> has $u(q)=\sum_s p_sv_s(q)$ and $u(q')=\sum_s p_sv_s(q')$. The assertion holds simultaneously for any number of such pairs.
  - Explanation: The interpolant has exactly the averaged values on pairs of opposite ratios.
- **claim-volume-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-e-volume-interpolation)): Let $w$ be centred, let $v$ be over $x$, and suppose $u\ge tw+(1-t)v$ on $R$ for $0<t<1$. For $f\ne0$ with $w(f)>0$, $$\label{eq:E-volume-interpolation} \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(u)\le t^{-n} \frac{tw(f)}{tw(f)+(1-t)v(f)}\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w).$$ If $v$ is also centred, $t=1/2$, and $v(f)\ge(1+\gamma)w(f)>0$ for $0<\gamma\le1$, then $$\label{eq:E-volume-gap} \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w)+\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(v)-2\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(u)\ge c(n,\gamma)\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w),$$ where $c(n,\gamma)>0$ depends only on $n,\gamma$. Consequently valuations whose volumes are within relative $o(1)$ of the minimum on the same $A=1$ common slice are multiplicatively $1+o(1)$ equal on *all* regular functions. The conclusion is uniform when the dimension is bounded. A sufficiently small fixed relative error similarly gives any prescribed fixed multiplicative closeness to one.
  - Explanation: Interpolation gives a volume bound and a strict gap, so near-minimizers are multiplicatively close.
- **claim-slice-boundary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-e-slice-boundary)): Choose positive rational numbers $\theta_j$ with $\sum_j\theta_j<1$. For sufficiently general scalars $a_j$, put $E_j=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(h^{m_j}+a_jF_j)$ and $$\label{eq:E-slice-boundary} \Psi=(1-\sum_j\theta_j)H+ \sum_j\frac{\theta_j}{m_j}E_j.$$ Then $\Psi$ is effective and lc, and its nonzero zero-discrepancy rays over the germ are exactly the common lc rays. On every such ray, $$\label{eq:E-pencil-order} v(h^{m_j}+a_jF_j)=m_jv(h).$$ In particular, the coefficients $\theta_j$ may be chosen arbitrarily small while retaining these conclusions.
  - Explanation: An lc boundary built from general pencil members encodes the common slice.
- **claim-exact-minimizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-e-exact-minimizer)): For every allowed height $b$, the homogeneous functional $b(v)\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(v)^{1/n}$ on centred common lc rays has a unique minimizing ray. Its $A=1$ representative depends continuously on the coefficients of $b$ while the height stays strictly positive on the fixed slice. It is fixed by every automorphism preserving the slice and the height.* *If the coefficients of $b$ are rational, its minimizer $w$ is the normalized-volume minimizer of an effective rational klt pair on $x\in S$. More precisely, one can choose an effective rational lc Cartier boundary $\Psi$ with exactly the common lc rays as its zero-discrepancy locus, and one positive rational number $\lambda$, fixed independently of the competing valuation, such that $$\label{eq:E-klt-perturbation} \Psi_\lambda=\Psi-\lambda b_0H +\lambda\sum_\ell b_\ell\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(G_\ell)$$ is effective and klt, and $w$ minimizes $\widehat{\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}}_{S,\Psi_\lambda}$ among all centred valuations. On the common lc rays its discrepancy is $\lambda b$.* *For the same rational height, the algebra $\operatorname{gr}_wR$ is finitely generated and normal. Its spectrum $S^0$ is a klt $\mathbb Q$-Gorenstein cone. Write $h^0,F_j^0,G_\ell^0$ for initial forms, $w^0$ for the grading valuation, and $\mathcal Q^0,b^0$ for the analogous central slice and height. Then $(S^0,\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(h^0))$ is lc, $A_{S^0}(w^0)=w^0(h^0)=1$, $w^0\in\mathcal Q^0$, and $b^0$ is strictly positive on $\mathcal Q^0$. The valuation $w^0$ minimizes $b^0\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}^{1/n}$ on its centred part.
  - Explanation: The height-volume functional has a unique minimizer that is realized as a normalized-volume minimizer of a rational klt pair.
- **claim-height-near-minimizers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-e-height-near-minimizers)): Fix an allowed height $b$ and normalize its common rays by $b=1$. If $w_i,v_i$ have functional values within relative $o(1)$ of the minimum, then $w_i=(1+o(1))v_i$ on all regular functions, with an error independent of the function. The same conclusion, after $A=1$ normalization, holds for minimizers of heights whose coefficients converge to those of $b$.
  - Explanation: Near-minimizers of a fixed height agree on all regular functions up to a small relative error.
- **claim-minimizer-domination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-e-domination)): For the minimizing ray $w$ and every $v\in\mathcal Q$, $$\label{eq:E-domination} \frac{v(f)}{b(v)}\le 2^n\frac{w(f)}{b(w)} \qquad(f\in R).$$ For rational heights the same statement holds on the central slice with all symbols carrying a superscript $0$.
  - Explanation: Every slice valuation is dominated by the minimizer up to a dimensional factor.
- **claim-one-sided-tilt** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-e-one-sided-tilt)): Let $G\in R\setminus\{0\}$, $m\in\mathbb Z_{>0}$, and $g(v)=v(G)/m-v(h)$. Suppose $g(v)/v(h)\ge2c$ on some common lc ray, where $c>0$ is rational. Set $$M=\max_{\mathcal Q}g\ge2c,\qquad \mu_*=(M-c)^{-1},\qquad b_\mu(v)=v(h)+\mu(cv(h)-g(v)).$$ For $0\le\mu<\mu_*$ the height is positive. There is a rational $\mu$ in this interval whose $A=1$ minimizing valuation $w$ satisfies $$g(w)>0,\qquad b_\mu(w)\ge1.$$ Moreover $b_\mu\le2+1/c$ on $\mathcal Q$, and the corresponding central height obeys the same bound on $\mathcal Q^0$.
  - Explanation: A rational tilt of the height forces the minimizer to have positive value on a given defect function.
- **claim-weighted-presentation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-f-presentation)): Choose $x_1,\ldots,x_N\in\mathfrak m_x$ whose symbols generate $\breve R$, and put $\alpha_i=w(x_i)>0$. Any prescribed finite list of functions of positive $w$-value can be included in this list. Then $$\label{eq:F-presentation} \widehat{\mathcal O}_{S,x}=\mathbb C[[X_1,\ldots,X_N]]/I, \qquad \breve R=\mathbb C[X_1,\ldots,X_N]/\operatorname{in}_{\alpha}I,$$ where initial forms have least weight. The valuation ideals are exactly the images of the ideals generated by monomials of weight at least the prescribed threshold. The same description holds before completion by intersection.* *For every sufficiently nearby linear functional $L:\Lambda_w\otimes\mathbb R\longrightarrow\mathbb R$, positive on the listed weights, the vector $\alpha_L=(L(\alpha_i))$ gives the same initial ideal, with its grading composed with $L$. Its filtration defines a centred quasi-monomial valuation $w_L$. The functionals can be chosen rational, and the resulting valuations can be computed monomially on a fixed smooth chart for $w$.
  - Explanation: The graded algebra yields a weighted presentation and rational regradings of the valuation.
- **claim-canonical-degeneration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-f-canonical-degeneration)): Under the hypotheses preceding Lemma <a href="#lem:F-presentation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:F-presentation">41</a>, $S^0$ has a divisor-free reflexive canonical generator $\omega^0$. It has the $G$-character of $\omega$. It is homogeneous for $\mathbb T_w$, and its weight $\kappa$ satisfies $$\label{eq:F-canonical-weight} \langle w,\kappa\rangle=A_S(w).$$ If an additional torus acts on the original germ, preserves $w$, and makes $\omega$ homogeneous, its character is preserved separately. None of these assertions assumes $A_S(w)=w(h)$. Moreover the action of $G$ on $S^0$ is quasi-étale.
  - Explanation: The degeneration carries a divisor-free canonical generator with the original character and weight equal to the discrepancy.
- **claim-lc-degeneration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-f-lc-degeneration)): If $w$ is $h$-lc, then $(S^0,\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\bar h))$ is lc and $\omega^0$ has the same fine torus weight and $G$-character as $\bar h$. Consequently $\omega^0/\bar h$ is invariant, and the quotient cone has the log-form data of Section <a href="#sec:D" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:D">4</a>. If $\Delta$ is any effective rational sum of principal divisors near $x$, with $(S,\Delta)$ lc and $A_S(w)=w(\Delta)$, its initial boundary $\Delta^0$ is lc on $S^0$. All these assertions also hold with specified additional equivariant gradings.
  - Explanation: For an h-lc valuation the initial boundary is lc and the canonical generator matches the weight of the initial function.
- **claim-sign-bridge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-f-bridge)): Suppose $w$ is common $h$-lc and $w(f_j)=0$ for every $j$. Let $g=U/h^b$, where $0\ne U\in\mathcal O_{S,x}$, $b\in\mathbb N_{>0}$, and $w(g)=0$. Let $\mathcal Q$ be the actual common slice and $\mathcal Q^0$ the common slice defined by $\bar h$ and the $\bar f_j$ on $S^0$. Then $$\label{eq:F-bridge} q(g)\le0\quad(q\in\mathcal Q) \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad q^0(\bar g)\le0\quad(q^0\in\mathcal Q^0).$$ One may add any finite list of tests of value zero at $w$. There is no bound on the degree or the cost of $g$ in this statement.
  - Explanation: Sign inequalities transfer exactly between the actual slice and the cone slice with no bound on degree or cost.
- **claim-exact-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-f-exact-tests)): Suppose $w$ is common $h$-lc. If every common actual valuation satisfies $q(f_j)=0$, then every common cone valuation satisfies $q^0(\bar f_j)=0$. The symbols $\bar f_j$ are invariant under $G$ and $\mathbb T_w$. Every nonzero pure weight valuation of the cone is $h$-lc and, after normalization by $\bar h=1$, is common. Pure weights on the boundary of the dual weight cone are allowed under the closed-centre convention.
  - Explanation: Exact test values and pure weights pass to the central cone.
- **claim-compression** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-f-compression)): Suppose $w$ is as above, is $h$-lc, and satisfies $w(f_j)=0$ for every $j$. Let $v$ be a centred $G\times\mathbb T_w$-invariant, $\bar h$-lc quasi-monomial valuation on $S^0$, with normal finitely generated klt graded algebra $R^{00}$. Suppose also $v(\bar f_j)=0$. The algebra $R^{00}$ has its joint grading by the old torus and the $v$-grading. For arbitrarily small positive $\delta$ outside a countable exceptional set, it is the associated graded of an actual centred quasi-monomial valuation $w_\delta$ on $S$, whose grading evaluates a joint degree $(a,b)$ as $a+\delta b$. The valuation $w_\delta$ is $h$-lc, is exact on all $f_j$, and, after $h=1$ normalization, tends multiplicatively to $w$ uniformly on all nonzero regular functions. If $v$ is nonzero on an old degree-zero rational function, the rational rank of $w_\delta$ is strictly larger than that of $w$.
  - Explanation: Two successive gradings are compressed to one actual valuation, strictly raising rational rank when the second is nonzero on an old degree-zero function.
- **claim-rank-refinement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-f-refinement)): Consider a sequence of the preceding data in dimensions at most $n_0$. Suppose that the common actual slices satisfy $q(f_j)=0$ identically, that $w$ is a centred point of the slice with the stipulated normal finitely generated klt graded algebra, and that the cones satisfy the following balance condition with one constant $C$ along the sequence: $$\label{eq:F-balance} q^0(s)\le Cw^0(s) \quad\begin{gathered} 0\ne s\in\breve R\text{ fine-homogeneous},\\[-2pt] q^0\text{ common},\quad q^0(\bar h)=w(h)=1. \end{gathered}$$ After passage to a subsequence, the $w$ can be replaced by $w'$ with all these properties, with a new fixed balance constant, and with $$\label{eq:F-refinement-comparison} w'(f)=(1+o(1))w(f) \qquad(0\ne f\in\mathcal O_{S,x}),$$ uniformly in $f$ in the normalization $w'(h)=w(h)=1$. On the angular model $V_{\mathbf u}$ of the new cone, the notation of Section <a href="#sec:D" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:D">4</a> then satisfies $$\label{eq:F-angular-order} T(\Gamma)\le(1+\eta_i)D(T),\qquad \eta_i\longrightarrow0,$$ for every effective $\mathbb Q$-divisor $\Gamma\sim_\mathbb QD$ and every primitive divisorial $T$ such that $a(T)=0$ and $T(\bar f_j)\ge0$ for all $j$. This is condition <span class="upright">(C-$\alpha$)</span> of Section <a href="#sec:C" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:C">3</a>. The initial symbols here refer to $w'$.* *The balance hypothesis holds when $w$ is the exact minimizer on the common slice for the height $q(h)$.
  - Explanation: Repeated refinement yields the angular order condition after boundedly many steps.
- **claim-pinning-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-g-pinning)): Fix an integer $n>0$ and $\epsilon>0$. There is no sequence of the following data satisfying all the conditions below. For each index $i$, let $x_i\in S_i$ be an $n$-dimensional complex klt germ with an action of a finite cyclic group $\Gamma_i$ fixing $x_i$, such that the quotient map is quasi-étale and $S_i/\Gamma_i$ is $\epsilon$-lc. Suppose $K_{S_i}$ has a nowhere-vanishing local eigen-generator $\omega_i$, and let $h_i$ be a nonzero regular eigenfunction with the same character $\chi_i$ as $\omega_i$. The character need not be faithful. Assume that $(S_i,\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(h_i))$ is lc and admits a centred quasi-monomial valuation $T_i$ with $$A_{S_i}(T_i)=T_i(h_i)=1.$$ The excluded additional condition is: for every fixed integer $m>0$, there is an index $i(m)$ such that, for all $i\ge i(m)$ and every regular semi-invariant $s$ of character $\chi_i^m$, $$\label{eq:G-input} T_i(s)\ge m.$$
  - Explanation: No sequence of the described cyclic quotient germs can satisfy the stated lower bound on semi-invariant values.
- **claim-noncancellation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-g-noncancellation)): At the preceding stage, consider any finite linear combination of monomials in the accumulated and newly added $Y_j^+$ and the current $G_a$, with nonnegative exponents. Suppose its least-$w$ group has a nonzero sum of initial symbols. If all its terms have the same value at an actual point of the pre-pinning slice, their sum has that value. This assertion holds eventually simultaneously in all polynomial degrees. Moreover, at a later working valuation on this slice where these terms are tied, the same non-cancellation assertion transfers to its cone at every common test where the terms remain tied and the pair values are opposite.
  - Explanation: Tied terms have a sum that attains the common value in every degree on the pre-pinning slice.
- **claim-product-growth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-g-product-growth)): Let $V$ be a finite-dimensional subspace of a function field over an algebraically closed field, containing $1$. Put $g=\dim V$ and $a=\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}k(V)\le n$. If $V^{(N)}$ is the span of $N$-fold products of members of $V$, there is $c_n>0$, depending only on $n$, such that $$\dim V^{(N)}\ge c_n gN^a \qquad(N\ge1).$$
  - Explanation: Iterated product spaces grow with a uniform lower bound in terms of dimension and transcendence degree.
- **claim-high-pin** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-g-high-pin)): Consider a high stage with pre-pinning slice, working valuation $w$, baseline height $\mathfrak B$, scale $L$, and $s$ selected values as above. After passage to a subsequence, finitely many invariant tests of cost $O(L)$ can be added with the following properties.* 1. *The resulting slice contains a centred valuation. The added tests are exact on that slice, all old tests and proportionalities remain in force, and the accumulated positive symbols remain algebraically independent at the exact minimizer and working valuation in <span class="upright">(iii)</span> below.* 2. *The selected values either vanish or lie on a single rational line. In the line case their proportions have the bounds <a href="#eq:G-proportions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:G-proportions">[eq:G-proportions]</a>, with $L_-=L$. An old anchor remains the anchor; if a new anchor is chosen, its scale is $L_1=L$.* 3. *The baseline height is retained unless an anchor is first chosen, in which case it may change to the rational height <a href="#eq:G-baseline" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:G-baseline">[eq:G-baseline]</a>. It satisfies <a href="#eq:G-baseline-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:G-baseline-bounds">[eq:G-baseline-bounds]</a> on the new actual slice. At its exact minimizer $v'$, and at an arbitrarily close rank refinement $w'$, the anchor, when present, satisfies $\|v'(Y_0^+)\|,\|w'(Y_0^+)\|\ge cL_1$. Both valuations dominate a fixed positive multiple of $w$ on every regular function. In particular, $$w'\ge c w.$$* *Consequently <a href="#it:G-tests" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="it:G-tests">[it:G-tests]</a>–<a href="#it:G-working" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="it:G-working">[it:G-working]</a> hold with $L_-=L$. The next-scale assertions <a href="#it:G-lattice" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="it:G-lattice">[it:G-lattice]</a>–<a href="#it:G-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="it:G-field">[it:G-field]</a> are not assumed in constructing the pin or its rank refinement.
  - Explanation: Finitely many tests of cost linear in the scale pin the selected values to a rational line while keeping a centred valuation.
- **claim-new-row** (theorem, [source](#stmt-clm-g-new-row)): After a subsequence there is an additional row of the form <a href="#eq:G-row" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:G-row">[eq:G-row]</a>, exact at $v$, whose transverse part is independent of the previously imposed transverse rows.
  - Explanation: An additional exact integral row exists whose transverse part is independent of earlier rows.
- **claim-pin-consequences** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-g-pin-consequences)): The working valuation $w'$ furnished by Proposition <a href="#prop:G-high-pin" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:G-high-pin">51</a> satisfies <a href="#it:G-lattice" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="it:G-lattice">[it:G-lattice]</a>–<a href="#it:G-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="it:G-field">[it:G-field]</a> at the next scale $L_-=L$.
  - Explanation: The pinned working valuation satisfies the next-scale lattice and field assertions.
- **claim-factorial-saturation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-h-saturation)): For each example there is a primitive divisorial order $w$ over $X$ whose image centre on $Z$ contains the specified point and for which $$\label{eq:H-saturation} w(\Delta_s)\ge k A_X(w)\quad (k>0,\ k\mid M_i),\qquad w(\Delta_{s_b})=bA_X(w).$$ After replacing the example by a geometric generic slice, one may assume that the image centre is a closed point $z$, that $1\le\dim X=\dim Z\le d$, and that all the inequalities hold for the complete local systems at $z$.
  - Explanation: A primitive divisorial order saturates the complete anti-canonical systems at the specified point.
- **claim-canonical-cover** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-h-canonical-cover)): Suppose $f$ is an isomorphism near $z$. There is a normal integral cyclic cover $S_0\to X$, with a unique point $o$ over $z$, a quasi-étale cyclic action fixing $o$, and a divisor-free canonical generator of character one. It has a regular eigenfunction $f_b=s_bt^b$ of character $b$ and a divisorial valuation $w_0$ centred at $o$ such that, with $A_0=A_{S_0}(w_0)>0$, $$\label{eq:H-upstairs-saturation} (S_0,\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(f_b)/b)\text{ is lc},\qquad w_0(f_b)=bA_0,\qquad w_0(a)\ge kA_0$$ for every regular eigenfunction $a$ of character $k$ when $k>0$ and $k\mid M_i$. The variety $S_0$ is klt.
  - Explanation: In the isomorphic case a cyclic canonical cover with one fixed point and an lc saturated valuation exists.
- **claim-signed-torsor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-h-signed-torsor)): Suppose $f$ is not an isomorphism near $z$. The signed algebra $$\label{eq:H-signed-algebra} R=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb Z} H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(-kK_X))t^k,\qquad S_0=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R,$$ over the affine base is a finitely generated normal domain of dimension $\dim X+1$. Write $R_k=H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(-kK_X))t^k$ for its homogeneous part of degree $k$. Its *positive chamber* is the open $$S_0^+=\bigcup_{k>0}\ \bigcup_{0\ne a\in R_k}D_{S_0}(a),$$ where $D_{S_0}(a)$ is the principal open on which $a$ does not vanish. This chamber has complement of codimension at least two. The form $$\label{eq:H-torsor-form} \Omega_0=t\omega_X\wedge d\log t$$ is a divisor-free canonical generator of grading weight one, and every finite scalar grading subgroup acts quasi-étalely.* *The variety $S_0$ is klt, and $(S_0,\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(s_bt^b)/b)$ is lc. There is a grading-fixed closed point $o$ over $z$ and a rational Gauss valuation $w_0$ centred there which satisfies <a href="#eq:H-upstairs-saturation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:H-upstairs-saturation">[eq:H-upstairs-saturation]</a> for every actual positive degree $k\mid M_i$. Moreover there are numbers $\alpha>0$ and $F_w>\alpha$, depending on the example, for which every nonzero homogeneous section satisfies $$\label{eq:H-two-slopes} w_0(s t^k)\ge \begin{cases} k\alpha,&k>0,\\ \|k\|(F_w-\alpha),&k<0. \end{cases}$$
  - Explanation: In the non-isomorphic case the signed anti-canonical algebra gives a klt torsor with a two-slope saturated valuation.
- **claim-padding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-h-padding)): In either construction, set $$S=S_0\times\mathbf A^{b-1}, \qquad (o,0)\in S,$$ and give every added coordinate $u_j$ character one. Then $S$ is klt, the finite quotient $Y=S/\mu_r$ is $\epsilon$-lc, and the canonical generator on $S$ has character $b$. There is a regular function $h$ of character $b$ such that $(S,\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(h))$ is lc and a centred quasi-monomial valuation $T_0$ such that $$\label{eq:H-padding-equality} A_S(T_0)=T_0(h)=bA_0.$$
  - Explanation: Adding weighted coordinates matches the canonical character with the complement degree and yields an lc slice with the actual quotient gap.
- **claim-pinning-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-h-g-input)): The failure sequence produces, after a subsequence, a fixed-dimensional sequence satisfying every hypothesis of Theorem <a href="#thm:G-pinning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:G-pinning">48</a>.
  - Explanation: A failure sequence produces data satisfying all hypotheses of the pinning obstruction.
- **claim-field-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-h-field-reduction)): It suffices to prove Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> over $\mathbb C$. More precisely, a uniform positive lower bound for the log canonical threshold of $\mathfrak m_z\mathcal O_X$ implies the Cartier-section assertion after replacing the bound by a smaller number less than one. Both the hypotheses and this ideal threshold are preserved by algebraically closed field extensions of characteristic zero.
  - Explanation: It suffices to prove the main theorem over the complex numbers via thresholds of point ideals.

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-a-linear-chamber",
        "exact_quote": "In the same setting suppose $a$ is an lc effective generalized CY over $S$, with values in $(1/p)\\mathbb Z$ for fixed $p$, and $a+t d$ for $0\\le t\\le t_0$ are effective generalized CYs, klt for positive $t$, $d\\ge0$ on all divisorials; take $L_t$ affine, and $t_0>0$ fixed rational. Then eventually there is a uniform smaller $t_1>0$ on which discriminants equal $a'+t d'$ with $a'$ of bounded denominator (i.e., the same $p'$ works on all models). There is also the following version with extra data:*\n\n1.  *$a+\\lambda h+s c$ are effective generalized CYs over $S$ for $0<\\lambda\\le\\lambda_0,\\ 0<s\\le\\lambda\\theta_0$, $\\lambda_0,\\theta_0>0$ fixed, $h$ of bounded denominator, $c\\ge0$;*\n\n2.  *on $a=0$, $d\\le c\\le C d$, $-\\eta_i c\\le h\\le Cc$ with fixed $C$ and $\\eta_i\\to0^+$.*\n\n*Then there are uniform $\\lambda_1,\\theta_1>0$, with $\\lambda_1\\le\\lambda_0$ and $\\theta_1\\le\\theta_0$, such that eventually, on the sector $0<\\lambda\\le\\lambda_1$, $0<s\\le\\lambda\\theta_1$, the data are klt generically over $S$, their discriminants are exactly $a'+\\lambda h'+s c'$, and $h'$ has bounded denominator. Moreover, eventually there is *no* divisorial $T$ horizontal over $S$ with $a(T)=0,\\ h(T)<0$. In particular for $S$ a point exact nonnegativity on $a=0$ follows even though only the approximate bound $-\\eta_i c$ was assumed.*\n\n*In this statement the minima are lexicographic: first minimize $a/e$, then $d/e$ on its minimum locus; for the extra data first minimize $a/e$, then $h/e$, then $c/e$. Here $e\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_P=T|_{k(S)}$. Thus $a'$ itself is the discriminant in discrepancy convention even if the endpoint is not klt generically. The family $a'+t d'$, *including its endpoint at zero*, has the generalized effective log data (with nef b-part and total logarithmic divisor $L_t$).",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-a-lc-place-tools",
        "exact_quote": "Let $g:X_1\\to W$ be a Mori step as above, and let $A_*$ be an effective generalized CY discrepancy on $X_1$ over $S$.*\n\n1.  *Suppose that $A_*$ is lc over the generic point of $W$, and that an effective generalized klt CY over $S$ is available for perturbation. Any prescribed horizontal divisorial lc place remains an lc place of an ordinary boundary $\\Omega$, effective on the generic fibre $F$, with $n(K_F+\\Omega_F)\\sim0$, for a bounded positive integer $n$.*\n\n2.  *For $P$ on a birational model of $W$, suppose $A_*$ is klt generically and $T$ computes the discriminant $b_*(P)$. There is an ordinary $n$-CY over the DVR, lc in its b-discrepancies and effective horizontally on $F$, with $n$ bounded, keeping $T$ as an lc place. Here $n$-CY means that the ordinary total log canonical divisor, multiplied by $n$, is linearly trivial over the trait. Moreover for the adjusted discrepancy $A_*-b_*(P)e$, any two minimizing divisorials can be joined by a chain/interpolation of lc places in the following sense: on a log resolution over the generic DVR their strict divisors (or extractions of them) belong to a connected snc floor consisting of vertical coefficient-one divisors with monomial cones at the intersections. One can include any additional finite discrepancy data in the resolution. In particular normalized evaluations $a/e$ of such other data interpolate continuously along the chain, using rational weights to approximate intermediate values by divisorial evaluations.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },

        {

      "kind": "theorem",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-a-uniform-charts",
        "exact_quote": "At a chosen vertical place in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:A-lc-place-tools\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:A-lc-place-tools\">4</a><span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>, after a bounded degree extension of $k(W)$ and a chosen extension of the DVR and of the place, there is a toroidal cone representing the place, with nonnegative coordinates $$\\label{eq:A-contact-cone}\n       (x,e_*),\\qquad M x=r e_* .$$ Here $M$ ranges over a finite list of integral matrices of bounded sizes, $r$ is integral (possibly huge), $e_*$ is value of the new uniformizer, and original uniformizer value is $\\rho e_*$ with positive bounded $\\rho$. Nonzero integral solutions on the cone give divisorial valuations (not necessarily primitive after restriction); evaluations of discrepancy data always refer to homogeneous values by restriction. For horizontal places as in the first bullet one just has an orthant on a bounded generic resolution, without $e_*$. For *any* effective generalized CY data $A$ on $X_1$ over $S$, values on these cones are finite continuous homogeneous piecewise linear, and are Lipschitz in $x$ with uniform constant *at fixed* $e_*>0$ (uniform homogeneous Lipschitz in the horizontal case). Constants depend on the boundedness in this Mori step and the complement indices, *not* on $r$, vertical coefficients, or the nef part index or the chosen data $A$. In particular finitely many differences at fixed parameter separations have uniform Lipschitz bounds as well.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Nonzero integral solutions on the cone give divisorial valuations (not necessarily primitive after restriction); evaluations of discrepancy data always refer to homogeneous values by restriction.       "explanation": "Toroidal cone charts give discrepancy functions that are piecewise linear with uniform Lipschitz bounds independent of the contact and nef-part index.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-a-toroidal-charts",
        "exact_quote": "For the prescribed place and tailored complement in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:A-uniform-charts\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:A-uniform-charts\">5</a>, there are charts with its stated contact equations and integral-valuation property. The prescribed place is monomial on one of these charts. They have rational functions $z_1,\\ldots,z_f$, $f=\\dim F$, with uniformly bounded pole degrees on a bounded embedding of $F$, such that:*\n\n1.  *their logarithmic differentials form a relative log basis in the vertical case and an absolute log basis over the generic field in the horizontal case;*\n\n2.  *their orders are linear functions of the chart coordinates with uniformly bounded slopes;*\n\n3.  *every rational monomial valuation in the vertical chart with $e_*>0$ restricts to a Gauss valuation on $K_1(z_1,\\ldots,z_f)$, where $K_1/k(W)$ is the bounded extension used in constructing the chart. Horizontally the same assertion holds with the trivial valuation on the generic field.*\n\n*Here Gauss means that evaluating a polynomial in the $z_j$ gives the minimum of its coefficient-adjusted monomial weights, without cancellation among terms of equal weight.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-a-toroidal-charts",
          "exact_quote": "For the prescribed place and tailored complement in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:A-uniform-charts\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:A-uniform-charts\">5</a>, there are charts with its stated contact equations and integral-valuation property. The prescribed place is monomial on one of these charts. They have rational functions $z_1,\\ldots,z_f$, $f=\\dim F$, with uniformly bounded pole degrees on a bounded embedding of $F$, such that:*\n\n1.  *their logarithmic differentials form a relative log basis in the vertical case and an absolute log basis over the generic field in the horizontal case;*\n\n2.  *their orders are linear functions of the chart coordinates with uniformly bounded slopes;*\n\n3.  *every rational monomial valuation in the vertical chart with $e_*>0$ restricts to a Gauss valuation on $K_1(z_1,\\ldots,z_f)$, where $K_1/k(W)$ is the bounded extension used in constructing the chart. Horizontally the same assertion holds with the trivial valuation on the generic field.*\n\n*Here Gauss means that evaluating a polynomial in the $z_j$ gives the minimum of its coefficient-adjusted monomial weights, without cancellation among terms of equal weight.",

        }

      "exact_statement": "For the prescribed place and tailored complement in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:A-uniform-charts\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:A-uniform-charts\">5</a>, there are charts with its stated contact equations and integral-valuation property. Horizontally the same assertion holds with the trivial valuation on the generic field.*\n\n*Here Gauss means that evaluating a polynomial in the $z_j$ gives the minimum of its coefficient-adjusted monomial weights, without cancellation among terms of equal weight.",
      "explanation": "Bounded charts carry coordinate functions of bounded pole degree on which monomial valuations restrict to Gauss valuations.",

      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-a-function-slopes",
        "exact_quote": "On a chart of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:A-toroidal-charts\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:A-toroidal-charts\">6</a>, the order of a nonzero rational function is continuous, homogeneous and finite piecewise linear, including on the faces. If its pole divisor on the embedded generic fibre has degree at most $m$, where $m\\ge1$, its Lipschitz constant in $x$ at fixed $e_*>0$ is at most $Cm$. Horizontally this bound holds on the entire cone. The constant $C$ depends only on the fixed families and chart choices. A function with pole degree less than one is constant on the generic fibre and has zero angular variation.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-a-horizontal-variants",
        "exact_quote": "The chart and metric conclusions have the following two variants.*\n\n1.  *The same tailorable bounded complement and homogeneous chart argument works if we are working over an algebraically closed generic field, with the relevant effective CYs on a $\\mathbb Q$-factorial model of Fano type, and have boundedness only up to isomorphism in codimension one of these models.*\n\n2.  *A Cartier rational b-order function $N$ with effective trace of degree $O(\\xi)$ on the bounded model, b-linearly $\\mathbb Q$-equivalent to a difference of b-nef parts both of trace degrees $O(\\xi)$, has horizontal Lipschitz constant $O(\\xi)$, including when $\\xi\\to0^+$.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-a-horizontal-variants",
          "exact_quote": "The chart and metric conclusions have the following two variants.*\n\n1.  *The same tailorable bounded complement and homogeneous chart argument works if we are working over an algebraically closed generic field, with the relevant effective CYs on a $\\mathbb Q$-factorial model of Fano type, and have boundedness only up to isomorphism in codimension one of these models.*\n\n2.  *A Cartier rational b-order function $N$ with effective trace of degree $O(\\xi)$ on the bounded model, b-linearly $\\mathbb Q$-equivalent to a difference of b-nef parts both of trace degrees $O(\\xi)$, has horizontal Lipschitz constant $O(\\xi)$, including when $\\xi\\to0^+$.",
          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The chart and metric conclusions have the following two variants.*\n\n1.  *The same tailorable bounded complement and homogeneous chart argument works if we are working over an algebraically closed generic field, with the relevant effective CYs on a $\\mathbb Q$-factorial model of Fano type, and have boundedness only up to isomorphism in codimension one of these models.*\n\n2.  *A Cartier rational b-order function $N$ with effective trace of degree $O(\\xi)$ on the bounded model, b-linearly $\\mathbb Q$-equivalent to a difference of b-nef parts both of trace degrees $O(\\xi)$, has horizontal Lipschitz constant $O(\\xi)$, including when $\\xi\\to0^+$.",
      "explanation": "The chart and metric conclusions persist in two horizontal variants.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-a-horizontal-variants",
          "exact_quote": "The chart and metric conclusions have the following two variants.*\n\n1.",
          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }

      "id": "claim-horizontal-variants",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-cor-a-horizontal-variants"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-a-lattice-approximation",
        "exact_quote": "1.  *Let $u_i$ lie in a fixed nonnegative orthant and $\\lvert u_i\\rvert\\to\\infty$. After passing to a subsequence there are $s_i\\to0^+$, nonzero nonnegative integral vectors $n_i$, and a fixed, possibly subsequence-dependent, $C_*$ such that $$\\label{eq:A-horizontal-approximation}\n     \\lvert n_i-s_i u_i\\rvert\\le C_*s_i,\n     \\qquad s_i\\lvert u_i\\rvert\\longrightarrow\\infty .$$*\n\n2.  *Let $M_i x_i=r_i$, where $x_i\\ge0$, $r_i$ is integral and $M_i$ belongs to a fixed finite list of integral matrices. If $\\alpha_i>0$ tends to zero, then after passing to a subsequence there are positive integers $E_i$, nonnegative integral $n_i$, and a fixed $C_*$ such that $$\\label{eq:A-vertical-approximation}\n     M_i n_i=r_i E_i,\\qquad\n     s_i=E_i\\alpha_i\\longrightarrow0^+,\\qquad\n     \\lvert n_i-E_i x_i\\rvert\\le C_*s_i .$$*\n\n\n3.  *For every sufficiently small fixed $\\gamma>0$, the same matching equations admit $n_i\\ge0$ integral and $1\\le E_i\\le J(\\gamma)$ with $$\\label{eq:A-bounded-dirichlet}\n     \\lvert n_i-E_i x_i\\rvert\\le\\gamma ,$$ where $J(\\gamma)$ is uniform in $i$ and in the contact vectors $r_i$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-claim-a-quantized-cells",
        "exact_quote": "The bounded-denominator functions $a,h$, when present, have only a bounded finite set of possible slopes in a horizontal chart, or in integral kernel coordinates on a vertical slice $e_*=1$. Their vertical affine constant terms belong to a rational grid with bounded denominator, although their magnitudes need not be bounded. Their common cells are cut out by a fixed finite set of possible normal vectors, with offsets of bounded denominator. They are pointed, their vertices have bounded denominator, and they satisfy a uniform polyhedral distance bound.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-b-phase-nonvanishing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(V_i,H_i)$ be a sequence in bounded dimension, where $V_i$ is projective of Fano type and $H_i$ is a nef and big $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier rational Weil divisor. Suppose that, for rational $t\\in[0,L_i]$, where $L_i\\to\\infty$, there are generalized data with discrepancies $a_i(t)$ such that $$\\label{eq:B-family}\n B_i(t)\\ge R(H_i),\\qquad\n K_{V_i}+B_i(t)+\\mathbf M_i(t)_{V_i}\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}tH_i,\n \\qquad \\mathbf M_i(t)\\text{ is b-nef and $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier}.$$ Assume that $a_i(0)$ is klt and that $a_i(t)$ is nondecreasing in $t$ at every divisorial valuation.*\n\n*Assume the following condition, also after restriction to any subsequence. For every choice of rational $t_i\\to\\infty$ in the parameter range, one can pass to a further subsequence and choose a fixed positive integer $q$ such that, for every sequence of primitive divisorial valuations $T_i$ with $a_i(t_i,T_i)\\to0$, the signed phases $\\theta_i(T_i)$ of $qH_i(T_i)$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:B-phase-hypothesis}\n -o\\bigl(a_i(t_i,T_i)\\bigr)\n \\le \\theta_i(T_i)\n \\le O\\bigl(a_i(t_i,T_i)\\bigr).$$ Then there is an integer $M>0$ such that for every $i$ some $m_i\\in\\{1,\\ldots,M\\}$ satisfies $|\\lfloor m_iH_i\\rfloor|\\ne\\varnothing$. Equivalently, after replacing $M$ by a common multiple, the same positive degree works for all $i$.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },

For every choice of rational $t_i\\to\\infty$ in the parameter range, one can pass to a further subsequence and choose a fixed positive integer $q$ such that, for every sequence of primitive divisorial valuations $T_i$ with $a_i(t_i,T_i)\\to0$, the signed phases $\\theta_i(T_i)$ of $qH_i(T_i)$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:B-phase-hypothesis}\n -o\\bigl(a_i(t_i,T_i)\\bigr)\n \\le \\theta_i(T_i)\n \\le O\\bigl(a_i(t_i,T_i)\\bigr).$$ Then there is an integer $M>0$ such that for every $i$ some $m_i\\in\\{1,\\ldots,M\\}$ satisfies $|\\lfloor m_iH_i\\rfloor|\\ne\\varnothing$.
