    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-b-seifert",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be projective normal, and let $H$ be a $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier rational divisor. There is a projective normal compactified Seifert bundle $p:Y\\to V$ with disjoint $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier zero and infinity sections $E_0,D$ and a rational fibre coordinate $z$ such that $$\\label{eq:B-bundle-identities}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(z)=p^*H+E_0-D,\n \\qquad\n K_Y+E_0+D=p^*\\bigl(K_V+R(H)\\bigr).$$ The second equality uses compatible canonical divisors. The divisor $D$ is relatively ample; if $H$ is nef, then $D$ is nef, and if $H$ is nef and big, then $D+p^*H$ is nef and big.*\n\n*In these identities, pullback of a rational Weil divisor along the equidimensional map $p$ is defined at the generic points of its prime components, using the multiplicities of their inverse images. This agrees with Cartier pullback whenever the divisor is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Orders of $H$, $E_0$, and $D$ on higher models use their Cartier b-pullbacks.*\n\n\n*Suppose $B\\ge R(H)$ and $\\mathbf M$ is a nef $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier b-divisor on $V$. Give $Y$ boundary $p^*(B-R(H))$ and nef part $p^*\\mathbf M$. Write $a$ for the generalized discrepancy on $V$ and $A$ for that on $Y$. If a primitive divisorial valuation $v$ of $k(Y)$ restricts nontrivially to $w$ on $k(V)$, then $$\\label{eq:B-bundle-discrepancy}\n A(v)=a(w)+|h(v)|+\\operatorname{def}(v),\n \\qquad\n h(v)=v(E_0)-v(D)\\equiv-w(H)\\pmod\\mathbb Z,$$ where $\\operatorname{def}(v)$ is a nonnegative integer. It vanishes precisely for the Gauss, or fibre-torus-invariant, valuations. If $w=0$, the primitive valuations are the ordinary places of the generic $\\mathbb P^1$, and have discrepancy $1$. Consequently klt base data give klt data on $Y$ without the two sections; adding both sections gives lc data whose non-klt locus is contained in the sections.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-b-seifert",

The divisor $D$ is relatively ample; if $H$ is nef, then $D$ is nef, and if $H$ is nef and big, then $D+p^*H$ is nef and big.*\n\n*In these identities, pullback of a rational Weil divisor along the equidimensional map $p$ is defined at the generic points of its prime components, using the multiplicities of their inverse images. This agrees with Cartier pullback whenever the divisor is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Orders of $H$, $E_0$, and $D$ on higher models use their Cartier b-pullbacks.*\n\n\n*Suppose $B\\ge R(H)$ and $\\mathbf M$ is a nef $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier b-divisor on $V$. Give $Y$ boundary $p^*(B-R(H))$ and nef part $p^*\\mathbf M$. Write $a$ for the generalized discrepancy on $V$ and $A$ for that on $Y$. If a primitive divisorial valuation $v$ of $k(Y)$ restricts nontrivially to $w$ on $k(V)$, then $$\\label{eq:B-bundle-discrepancy}\n A(v)=a(w)+|h(v)|+\\operatorname{def}(v),\n \\qquad\n h(v)=v(E_0)-v(D)\\equiv-w(H)\\pmod\\mathbb Z,$$ where $\\operatorname{def}(v)$ is a nonnegative integer. It vanishes precisely for the Gauss, or fibre-torus-invariant, valuations. If $w=0$, the primitive valuations are the ordinary places of the generic $\\mathbb P^1$, and have discrepancy $1$. Consequently klt base data give klt data on $Y$ without the two sections; adding both sections gives lc data whose non-klt locus is contained in the sections.",
          "source_id": "PM-066.2"

      "exact_statement": "Let $V$ be projective normal, and let $H$ be a $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier rational divisor. The divisor $D$ is relatively ample; if $H$ is nef, then $D$ is nef, and if $H$ is nef and big, then $D+p^*H$ is nef and big.*\n\n*In these identities, pullback of a rational Weil divisor along the equidimensional map $p$ is defined at the generic points of its prime components, using the multiplicities of their inverse images.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-b-denominator-clearing",
        "exact_quote": "In bounded dimension, suppose $V$ is projective of Fano type and carries effective generalized klt Calabi–Yau data with $B\\ge R(H)$. After multiplying $H$ by a bounded positive integer, there is an ordinary klt log Fano boundary $\\Theta\\ge R(H)$. For this scaled divisor the Seifert bundle is of Fano type. The assertion does not require a bound on the Cartier index of the nef b-divisor.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-b-unbounded-index",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F_i$ be projective of positive bounded dimension and let $G_i$ be ample $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier rational divisors such that $(F_i,R(G_i))$ is klt and $$-(K_{F_i}+R(G_i))\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}I_iG_i,\n \\qquad I_i>0,\\quad I_i\\longrightarrow\\infty.$$ Let $C_i$ be the compactified Seifert cone obtained by contracting the zero section, and denote its infinity divisor by $D_i$. Then $C_i$ is klt of Fano type, $D_i$ is ample, and $-K_{C_i}\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}(I_i+1)D_i$. It is impossible that $$\\label{eq:B-cone-small-height}\n v_i(D_i)=o\\bigl(A_{C_i}(v_i)\\bigr)$$ for every sequence of primitive divisorial valuations with $A_{C_i}(v_i)\\to0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Then $C_i$ is klt of Fano type, $D_i$ is ample, and $-K_{C_i}\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}(I_i+1)D_i$.           "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $F_i$ be projective of positive bounded dimension and let $G_i$ be ample $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier rational divisors such that $(F_i,R(G_i))$ is klt and $$-(K_{F_i}+R(G_i))\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}I_iG_i,\n \\qquad I_i>0,\\quad I_i\\longrightarrow\\infty.$$ Let $C_i$ be the compactified Seifert cone obtained by contracting the zero section, and denote its infinity divisor by $D_i$. Then $C_i$ is klt of Fano type, $D_i$ is ample, and $-K_{C_i}\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}(I_i+1)D_i$. It is impossible that $$\\label{eq:B-cone-small-height}\n v_i(D_i)=o\\bigl(A_{C_i}(v_i)\\bigr)$$ for every sequence of primitive divisorial valuations with $A_{C_i}(v_i)\\to0$.",
      "explanation": "On a cone with unbounded index the height at infinity cannot be negligible against small discrepancies.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-b-principal-order",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F_i$ be projective of Fano type in bounded dimension, and let $P_i\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$ be $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier rational divisors. Suppose that $F_i$ carries effective generalized klt Calabi–Yau data with discrepancies $a_i$ and boundary at least $R(P_i)$. Suppose, after a fixed integral scaling if necessary, that the signed phases of $P_i$ at every sequence with $a_i(T_i)\\to0$ lie in $[-o(a_i(T_i)),O(a_i(T_i))]$. Then the least positive integer $q_i$ for which $q_iP_i$ is principal is bounded.*\n\n*More generally, the same cyclic-cover argument applies whenever the induced isotrivial fibration below has $u=o(A)$ at its small-discrepancy divisors over zero. This formulation permits the argument to be used after an independent estimate of the height $u$.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-c-cutoff-claims",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\dim W_\\beta>0$. After passage to a subsequence there is a fixed $\\delta>0$ and a projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial Fano type extraction $\\widehat W\\to W_\\beta$ such that $\\widehat W$ has ordinary $\\delta$-lc singularities and every positive coefficient of the pullback of $D_W/\\beta$ is at least $\\delta$. A bounded constant $C$ can be chosen so that $S(CD_W/\\beta)$ generates $k(W_\\beta)$.*\n\n*There are algebraically independent functions $\\phi_1,\\ldots,\\phi_{\\dim W_\\beta}$ in this space and a b-nef $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier order function $H$ on $k(W_\\beta)$, pulled back to $k(V)$, with uniformly bounded denominator of its values on primitive orders, such that:*\n\n1.  *If $a(T)=0$, then $H(T)\\leq CD(T)/\\beta$. If $T$ is nontrivial on $K_\\beta$ and all $T(\\phi_j)\\geq0$, then $H(T)\\leq-1$.*\n\n2.  *If $D(Q)=0$ and $a(Q)\\leq1$, then $H(Q)\\leq0$.*\n\n3.  *If $a(T)=0$ and <a href=\"#eq:C-alpha\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:C-alpha\">[eq:C-alpha]</a> holds at $T$, then $H(T)\\geq-C\\eta D(T)/\\beta$.*\n\n*In particular, if <a href=\"#eq:C-alpha\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:C-alpha\">[eq:C-alpha]</a> holds on all of $I_\\beta$ with $\\eta_i\\to0$, those primes are eventually horizontal over $K_\\beta$; that is, their restrictions to $K_\\beta$ are trivial.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-c-scaled-phase",
        "exact_quote": "Assume <a href=\"#eq:C-alpha\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:C-alpha\">[eq:C-alpha]</a> with $\\eta_i\\to0$ whenever $a(T)=0$ and $LD(T)\\to0$, along arbitrary subsequences. Let $\\ell$ be a rational principal order. Suppose that for every nonfloor prime $Q$ on $V$ there is an integer $p_Q$ with $$\\label{eq:C-nonfloor-phase}\n |p_Q-\\ell(Q)|\\leq G(Q),\\qquad\n G\\geq0,\\qquad G\\sim_\\mathbb Qc_gLD,$$ where $c_g\\geq0$ is rational and uniformly bounded. There is a fixed positive integer $q$ such that $$\\label{eq:C-scaled-phase}\n \\operatorname{dist}(q\\ell(T),\\mathbb Z)=O(LD(T))\n \\quad\\text{if }a(T)=0\\text{ and }LD(T)\\longrightarrow0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"

There is a fixed positive integer $q$ such that $$\\label{eq:C-scaled-phase}\n \\operatorname{dist}(q\\ell(T),\\mathbb Z)=O(LD(T))\n \\quad\\text{if }a(T)=0\\text{ and }LD(T)\\longrightarrow0.$$",

There is a fixed positive integer $q$ such that $$\\label{eq:C-scaled-phase}\n \\operatorname{dist}(q\\ell(T),\\mathbb Z)=O(LD(T))\n \\quad\\text{if }a(T)=0\\text{ and }LD(T)\\longrightarrow0.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-c-saturation-count",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f_1,\\ldots,f_s$ be a finite list of genuine rational functions on $V$, whose length may vary with the sequence, and let $$\\label{eq:C-neutral-polarization}\n J(T)=\\max\\{0,\\max_j(-T(f_j))\\},\\qquad\n 0\\leq J\\leq C L_0\\mathbf D,\\qquad1\\leq L_0=O(L).$$ Thus $J$ is an integral basepoint-free Cartier b-divisor on a high model. Suppose <a href=\"#eq:C-alpha\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:C-alpha\">[eq:C-alpha]</a> holds uniformly with $\\eta_i\\to0$ on $$\\mathcal L=\\{T:a(T)=0,\\ J(T)=0\\}.$$ In addition, on every subsequence on which $L_0\\to\\infty$, assume that all valuations in $\\mathcal L$ eventually restrict trivially to the *field generated by* $S(L_0D)$.*\n\n*After passage to a subsequence, there are cutoff fields $K_*$ of fixed transcendence degree $p$, a fixed constant $B$, and positive real numbers $v_i$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For every fixed $C_1>0$, eventually $S(C_1LD)\\subset K_*$.*\n\n2.  *Eventually $S(BLD)$ generates $K_*$. If $T\\in\\mathcal L$ is nonnegative on this space, its restriction to $K_*$ is trivial.*\n\n\n3.  *There is $C'>0$, independent of the positive integer $n$, such that for every fixed $n$, eventually $$\\label{eq:C-uniform-counts}\n     \\dim S(nLD)\\leq C'v_i n^p,\\qquad\n     \\dim\\operatorname{Span}\\{g_1\\cdots g_n:g_j\\in S(BLD)\\}\n                                          \\geq v_i n^p.$$*\n\n*The eventual index in <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> may depend on $C_1$, and that in <span class=\"upright\">(iii)</span> may depend on $n$; $B$ and $C'$ are fixed before $n$. All assertions concern divisorial evaluations. Exact triviality also holds on rational monomial subcones cut out by the indicated conditions.",

Suppose <a href=\"#eq:C-alpha\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:C-alpha\">[eq:C-alpha]</a> holds uniformly with $\\eta_i\\to0$ on $$\\mathcal L=\\{T:a(T)=0,\\ J(T)=0\\}.$$ In addition, on every subsequence on which $L_0\\to\\infty$, assume that all valuations in $\\mathcal L$ eventually restrict trivially to the *field generated by* $S(L_0D)$.*\n\n*After passage to a subsequence, there are cutoff fields $K_*$ of fixed transcendence degree $p$, a fixed constant $B$, and positive real numbers $v_i$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For every fixed $C_1>0$, eventually $S(C_1LD)\\subset K_*$.*\n\n2.  *Eventually $S(BLD)$ generates $K_*$.       "exact_statement": "Let $f_1,\\ldots,f_s$ be a finite list of genuine rational functions on $V$, whose length may vary with the sequence, and let $$\\label{eq:C-neutral-polarization}\n J(T)=\\max\\{0,\\max_j(-T(f_j))\\},\\qquad\n 0\\leq J\\leq C L_0\\mathbf D,\\qquad1\\leq L_0=O(L).$$ Thus $J$ is an integral basepoint-free Cartier b-divisor on a high model.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-c-orbifold-vanishing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal X$ be a smooth proper Deligne–Mumford stack over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, with trivial generic stabilizer and projective coarse space. Let $\\Theta$ be an effective rational divisor with simple normal crossing support and coefficients less than one. If $D$ is an integral Cartier divisor and $D-(K_{\\mathcal X}+\\Theta)$ is nef and big, then $H^q(\\mathcal X,\\mathcal O_{\\mathcal X}(D))=0$ for $q>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-c-adjoint-lattice",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial variety of Fano type of dimension $p>0$, and let $N$ be a nef and big integral Weil divisor. Suppose that $H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(N))$ defines a birational map. Choose an effective rational boundary $\\Delta$ with $(X,\\Delta)$ klt and $K_X+\\Delta\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$. At a general smooth point outside the supports of $N$ and $\\Delta$, fix regular parameters and take total-degree-then-lexicographic Taylor initial exponents, using the compatible local trivializations of the powers of $N$. Let $$K=\\overline{\\operatorname{conv}}\n \\left\\{\\frac{\\nu(s)}j:\n j\\in\\mathbb Z_{>0},\\quad\n 0\\ne s\\in H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(jN))\\right\\}.$$ If $t$ is a positive integer and $\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}^p$ satisfies $\\alpha+\\mathbf1\\in\\operatorname{int}(tK)$, then some section of $tN$ has initial exponent $\\alpha$.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-c-adjoint-lattice",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial variety of Fano type of dimension $p>0$, and let $N$ be a nef and big integral Weil divisor. Suppose that $H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(N))$ defines a birational map. Choose an effective rational boundary $\\Delta$ with $(X,\\Delta)$ klt and $K_X+\\Delta\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$. At a general smooth point outside the supports of $N$ and $\\Delta$, fix regular parameters and take total-degree-then-lexicographic Taylor initial exponents, using the compatible local trivializations of the powers of $N$. Let $$K=\\overline{\\operatorname{conv}}\n \\left\\{\\frac{\\nu(s)}j:\n j\\in\\mathbb Z_{>0},\\quad\n 0\\ne s\\in H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(jN))\\right\\}.$$ If $t$ is a positive integer and $\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}^p$ satisfies $\\alpha+\\mathbf1\\in\\operatorname{int}(tK)$, then some section of $tN$ has initial exponent $\\alpha$.",
          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $X$ be a projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial variety of Fano type of dimension $p>0$, and let $N$ be a nef and big integral Weil divisor. Suppose that $H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(N))$ defines a birational map. Choose an effective rational boundary $\\Delta$ with $(X,\\Delta)$ klt and $K_X+\\Delta\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$. At a general smooth point outside the supports of $N$ and $\\Delta$, fix regular parameters and take total-degree-then-lexicographic Taylor initial exponents, using the compatible local trivializations of the powers of $N$. Let $$K=\\overline{\\operatorname{conv}}\n \\left\\{\\frac{\\nu(s)}j:\n j\\in\\mathbb Z_{>0},\\quad\n 0\\ne s\\in H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(jN))\\right\\}.$$ If $t$ is a positive integer and $\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}^p$ satisfies $\\alpha+\\mathbf1\\in\\operatorname{int}(tK)$, then some section of $tN$ has initial exponent $\\alpha$.",
      "explanation": "Interior lattice points of the jet body are realized as Taylor initial exponents of sections.",

        "exact_quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:C-adjoint-lattice\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:C-adjoint-lattice\">20</a>, $K$ is bounded and contains the standard simplex $\\Sigma=\\operatorname{conv}(0,e_1,\\ldots,e_p)$. For every integer $n\\ge1$, $$h^0(X,nN)\\le(n+p)^p\\operatorname{vol}(K).$$ Fix any integer $t>1+2p$.         "anchor": "stmt-cor-c-neutral-field",
        "exact_quote": "In Lemma <a href=\"#lem:C-saturation-count\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:C-saturation-count\">18</a>, let $g_{i,1},\\ldots,g_{i,r_i}$ be any finite list contained in $S(C_1L_iD_i)$ for one fixed $C_1$. For all sufficiently large $i$, the entire field $F_i=k(g_{i,1},\\ldots,g_{i,r_i})$, and its relative algebraic closure inside $k(V_i)$, lie in $K_{*,i}$. Thus every test from part <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> is exactly zero on every nonzero element of these fields at that same index, without a degree-dependent eventual qualification. More generally, a valuation trivial on a subfield remains trivial on any algebraic extension of it inside its valued overfield.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-d-cone-dictionary",
        "exact_quote": "In the above setting the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *The discrepancy of $(X,H)$ at $(P,y)$ is $a(P)$, and its discrepancy at a pure weight is zero. Thus $a(P)$ is a nonnegative integer for every primitive $P$, and $\\mathbf u\\in\\operatorname{int}C$.*\n\n\n\n2.  *For rational $\\mu\\in\\operatorname{int}C$, the ray quotient $V_\\mu$ is normal and projective, with function field $\\mathcal K$. The order function $$\\label{eq:D-polarization}\n     \\mathbf L(\\mu)(P)=\\min_{y\\in\\Delta_P}\\langle y,\\mu\\rangle$$ is the b-pullback of an ample rational divisor on $V_\\mu$. Consequently $$\\label{eq:D-height-divisor}\n     \\mathbf D(P)=\\min_{y\\in\\Delta_P}(P,y)(h)\n                 =\\gamma(P)+\\mathbf L(\\mathbf u)(P)$$ is the b-pullback of an effective ample rational divisor $D$ on $V_{\\mathbf u}$.*\n\n\n\n3.  *At every actual prime $P$ of $V_\\mu$, a minimizer $y$ of <a href=\"#eq:D-polarization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:D-polarization\">[eq:D-polarization]</a> can be chosen so that $e(P,y)$ is a primitive prime order on $X$, for some positive integer $e$. For this choice, $$\\label{eq:D-actual-prime}\n     a(P)+(P,y)(h)=\\frac1e,\n     \\qquad e y\\in\\operatorname{Hom}(M,\\mathbb Z).$$ On each $V_\\mu$, $a$ is the discrepancy function of an effective ordinary lc Calabi–Yau pair, and $a+\\mathbf D$ is that of an effective generalized klt Calabi–Yau pair with nef part $\\mathbf D$. Each $V_\\mu$ is of Fano type. At an actual prime outside the floor $\\{a=0\\}$, the same minimizer satisfies $$\\label{eq:D-off-floor}\n     a(P)=1,\\qquad e=1,\\qquad (P,y)(h)=0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },

        {

Each $V_\\mu$ is of Fano type. At an actual prime outside the floor $\\{a=0\\}$, the same minimizer satisfies $$\\label{eq:D-off-floor}\n     a(P)=1,\\qquad e=1,\\qquad (P,y)(h)=0.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-d-lc-tests",
        "exact_quote": "Every nontrivial divisorial valuation nonnegative on $R$ and having zero discrepancy for $(X,H)$ is Gauss. Its restriction to $\\mathcal K$ is either $eP$ with $a(P)=0$, or trivial, in which case it is a nonzero pure weight in $\\sigma$. Conversely every rational Gauss extension with $a(P)=0$, and every nonzero rational pure weight, has zero discrepancy and a center whose closure contains the contracting vertex.*\n\n*If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}\\breve R\\to X$ is quasi-étale and the pairs are related by log pullback, every divisorial $h$-lc test upstairs restricts to one of these tests, up to scaling. Conversely all the rational tests just listed lift to divisorial $h$-lc tests upstairs whose center closures contain the upstairs vertex. These statements suffice in particular for testing invariant rational functions.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-d-lc-tests",

Conversely all the rational tests just listed lift to divisorial $h$-lc tests upstairs whose center closures contain the upstairs vertex. These statements suffice in particular for testing invariant rational functions.",
          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-d-section-symbols",
        "exact_quote": "For every positive integer $q$ and every $g\\in\\mathcal K$, $$\\label{eq:D-symbol-equivalence}\n g\\in S(q\\mathbf J_m)\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n g\\xi^q h^{qm}\\in\\breve R.$$ In particular the neutral functions of cost $b\\in\\mathbb N$ are precisely $$\\label{eq:D-neutral-functions}\n \\{g\\in\\mathcal K:g h^b\\in\\breve R\\}=S(b\\mathbf D).$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-d-sandwich",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L\\ge1$ vary with the sequence. Choose integers $m>m_0$ with $m-m_0\\ge cL$ for a fixed $c>0$, and suppose both $k+m_0\\mathbf u$ and $k+m\\mathbf u$ are interior. Assume that whenever primitive divisorial valuations $P$ satisfy $a(P)=0$ and $L\\mathbf D(P)\\to0$, there are integers $j_P$ such that $$\\label{eq:D-sandwich-hypothesis}\n j_P\\le\\mathbf J_{m_0}(P)\\le\\mathbf J_m(P)\n       \\le j_P+O(L\\mathbf D(P)).$$ Then there is a bounded positive integer $q$ and a nonzero $g\\in S(q\\mathbf J_m)$. Equivalently, $g\\xi^qh^{qm}$ is regular on $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}\\breve R$.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-d-regularization",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that the order condition <a href=\"#eq:C-alpha\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:C-alpha\">[eq:C-alpha]</a> on $V_{\\mathbf u}$, with error $\\eta_i\\to0$, holds whenever $a(P)=0$ and $L\\mathbf D(P)\\to0$. Fix $C_0>0$. If $k\\in M$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:D-width}\n |\\langle y,k\\rangle|\\le C_0L\\langle y,\\mathbf u\\rangle\n \\qquad(y\\in\\sigma),$$ there are a bounded positive integer $q$, a nonzero invariant rational eigenfunction $Y=fz^{qk}$, and a positive integer $b\\le C_2L$ such that $h^bY\\in\\breve R$. The constants are uniform in $k$. Both signs of $k$ may be treated with the same bounded multiplier, and their costs may be increased to a common cost $O(L)$.*\n\n*The same bounds hold uniformly if $L$ is replaced by any $L'\\ge L$ and <a href=\"#eq:D-width\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:D-width\">[eq:D-width]</a> is imposed at scale $L'$.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-d-regularization",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that the order condition <a href=\"#eq:C-alpha\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:C-alpha\">[eq:C-alpha]</a> on $V_{\\mathbf u}$, with error $\\eta_i\\to0$, holds whenever $a(P)=0$ and $L\\mathbf D(P)\\to0$. Fix $C_0>0$. If $k\\in M$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:D-width}\n |\\langle y,k\\rangle|\\le C_0L\\langle y,\\mathbf u\\rangle\n \\qquad(y\\in\\sigma),$$ there are a bounded positive integer $q$, a nonzero invariant rational eigenfunction $Y=fz^{qk}$, and a positive integer $b\\le C_2L$ such that $h^bY\\in\\breve R$. The constants are uniform in $k$. Both signs of $k$ may be treated with the same bounded multiplier, and their costs may be increased to a common cost $O(L)$.*\n\n*The same bounds hold uniformly if $L$ is replaced by any $L'\\ge L$ and <a href=\"#eq:D-width\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:D-width\">[eq:D-width]</a> is imposed at scale $L'$.",
          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose that the order condition <a href=\"#eq:C-alpha\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:C-alpha\">[eq:C-alpha]</a> on $V_{\\mathbf u}$, with error $\\eta_i\\to0$, holds whenever $a(P)=0$ and $L\\mathbf D(P)\\to0$. Fix $C_0>0$. If $k\\in M$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:D-width}\n |\\langle y,k\\rangle|\\le C_0L\\langle y,\\mathbf u\\rangle\n \\qquad(y\\in\\sigma),$$ there are a bounded positive integer $q$, a nonzero invariant rational eigenfunction $Y=fz^{qk}$, and a positive integer $b\\le C_2L$ such that $h^bY\\in\\breve R$. The constants are uniform in $k$. Both signs of $k$ may be treated with the same bounded multiplier, and their costs may be increased to a common cost $O(L)$.*\n\n*The same bounds hold uniformly if $L$ is replaced by any $L'\\ge L$ and <a href=\"#eq:D-width\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:D-width\">[eq:D-width]</a> is imposed at scale $L'$.",
      "explanation": "Characters of bounded width are regularized by a bounded multiplier at cost linear in the scale.",

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-d-character-count",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:C-saturation-count\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:C-saturation-count\">18</a> on $V_{\\mathbf u}$, and use its notation $p$ and $v_i$. For a fixed $C_0>0$, let $W_{t,k}$ be the space of regular symbols in $\\breve R$ of finite-group character $\\chi^t$ and torus weight $t\\mathbf u+k$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-d-tilted-characters",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose there are boundedly many invariant rational eigenfunctions $Y_j^\\pm$ of weights $\\pm k_j$, with $$h^{m_j}Y_j^\\pm\\in\\breve R,\\qquad\n 0<m_j\\le A L_j,\\qquad 1\\le L_j\\le L,$$ where $A$ is fixed. Fix bounded real numbers $u_j$ and $\\delta>0$. Assume that, at every nonzero pure weight and at all Gauss extensions over primitive $P$ with $a(P)=0$, $L\\mathbf D(P)\\to0$, the evaluations satisfy $$\\label{eq:D-tilt-hypothesis}\n y_j=v(Y_j^+)=-v(Y_j^-),\\qquad\n \\sum_j\\frac{u_jy_j}{L_j}\\le\\delta v(h).$$ It suffices to check these inequalities on rational vectors. For fixed $M_0$ sufficiently large compared with $1/\\delta$, choose integers $$r_j=M_0u_jL/L_j+O(1),$$ with uniformly bounded rounding errors. Then there is a regular nonzero symbol of finite-group character $\\chi^{qm}$ and torus weight $$\\label{eq:D-tilt-weight}\n q\\left(m\\mathbf u-\\sum_jr_jk_j\\right),\n \\qquad 0<m\\le C_6M_0\\delta L,$$ for a bounded positive integer $q$. The constant $C_6$ depends only on the fixed bounds for the number of functions, their costs, the $u_j$, and rounding errors; it is independent of $\\delta$ and $M_0$. The bound for $q$ may depend on the fixed choices $\\delta,M_0$. No order condition <a href=\"#eq:C-alpha\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:C-alpha\">[eq:C-alpha]</a> is assumed here.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-e-log-jet",
        "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many regular affine functions on $S$. On a fixed resolution $P$, with $F$ reduced snc, there is a constant $C$ such that, for every polynomial $g\\ne0$ of degree at most $D$ in those functions and every quasi-monomial $v$ over $x$, $$\\label{eq:E-jet}\n r_F(v)(g)\\le v(g)\\le r_F(v)(g)+C(D+1)A_{P,F}(v).$$ The same constant works when the centre is not closed and when some weights disappear on passing to a face of the monomial cone.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix finitely many regular affine functions on $S$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-e-volume-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $w$ be centred, let $v$ be over $x$, and suppose $u\\ge tw+(1-t)v$ on $R$ for $0<t<1$. For $f\\ne0$ with $w(f)>0$, $$\\label{eq:E-volume-interpolation}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(u)\\le t^{-n}\n   \\frac{tw(f)}{tw(f)+(1-t)v(f)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(w).$$ If $v$ is also centred, $t=1/2$, and $v(f)\\ge(1+\\gamma)w(f)>0$ for $0<\\gamma\\le1$, then $$\\label{eq:E-volume-gap}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(w)+\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(v)-2\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(u)\\ge c(n,\\gamma)\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(w),$$ where $c(n,\\gamma)>0$ depends only on $n,\\gamma$. Consequently valuations whose volumes are within relative $o(1)$ of the minimum on the same $A=1$ common slice are multiplicatively $1+o(1)$ equal on *all* regular functions. The conclusion is uniform when the dimension is bounded. A sufficiently small fixed relative error similarly gives any prescribed fixed multiplicative closeness to one.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-e-slice-boundary",
        "exact_quote": "Choose positive rational numbers $\\theta_j$ with $\\sum_j\\theta_j<1$. For sufficiently general scalars $a_j$, put $E_j=\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(h^{m_j}+a_jF_j)$ and $$\\label{eq:E-slice-boundary}\n \\Psi=(1-\\sum_j\\theta_j)H+\n                     \\sum_j\\frac{\\theta_j}{m_j}E_j.$$ Then $\\Psi$ is effective and lc, and its nonzero zero-discrepancy rays over the germ are exactly the common lc rays. On every such ray, $$\\label{eq:E-pencil-order}\n v(h^{m_j}+a_jF_j)=m_jv(h).$$ In particular, the coefficients $\\theta_j$ may be chosen arbitrarily small while retaining these conclusions.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-e-exact-minimizer",
        "exact_quote": "For every allowed height $b$, the homogeneous functional $b(v)\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(v)^{1/n}$ on centred common lc rays has a unique minimizing ray. Its $A=1$ representative depends continuously on the coefficients of $b$ while the height stays strictly positive on the fixed slice. It is fixed by every automorphism preserving the slice and the height.*\n\n\n*If the coefficients of $b$ are rational, its minimizer $w$ is the normalized-volume minimizer of an effective rational klt pair on $x\\in S$. More precisely, one can choose an effective rational lc Cartier boundary $\\Psi$ with exactly the common lc rays as its zero-discrepancy locus, and one positive rational number $\\lambda$, fixed independently of the competing valuation, such that $$\\label{eq:E-klt-perturbation}\n \\Psi_\\lambda=\\Psi-\\lambda b_0H\n                       +\\lambda\\sum_\\ell b_\\ell\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(G_\\ell)$$ is effective and klt, and $w$ minimizes $\\widehat{\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}}_{S,\\Psi_\\lambda}$ among all centred valuations. On the common lc rays its discrepancy is $\\lambda b$.*\n\n*For the same rational height, the algebra $\\operatorname{gr}_wR$ is finitely generated and normal. Its spectrum $S^0$ is a klt $\\mathbb Q$-Gorenstein cone. Write $h^0,F_j^0,G_\\ell^0$ for initial forms, $w^0$ for the grading valuation, and $\\mathcal Q^0,b^0$ for the analogous central slice and height. Then $(S^0,\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(h^0))$ is lc, $A_{S^0}(w^0)=w^0(h^0)=1$, $w^0\\in\\mathcal Q^0$, and $b^0$ is strictly positive on $\\mathcal Q^0$. The valuation $w^0$ minimizes $b^0\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}^{1/n}$ on its centred part.",

      },

        "exact_quote": "Fix an allowed height $b$ and normalize its common rays by $b=1$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-e-one-sided-tilt",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G\\in R\\setminus\\{0\\}$, $m\\in\\mathbb Z_{>0}$, and $g(v)=v(G)/m-v(h)$. Suppose $g(v)/v(h)\\ge2c$ on some common lc ray, where $c>0$ is rational. Set $$M=\\max_{\\mathcal Q}g\\ge2c,\\qquad\n \\mu_*=(M-c)^{-1},\\qquad\n b_\\mu(v)=v(h)+\\mu(cv(h)-g(v)).$$ For $0\\le\\mu<\\mu_*$ the height is positive. There is a rational $\\mu$ in this interval whose $A=1$ minimizing valuation $w$ satisfies $$g(w)>0,\\qquad b_\\mu(w)\\ge1.$$ Moreover $b_\\mu\\le2+1/c$ on $\\mathcal Q$, and the corresponding central height obeys the same bound on $\\mathcal Q^0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-e-one-sided-tilt",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G\\in R\\setminus\\{0\\}$, $m\\in\\mathbb Z_{>0}$, and $g(v)=v(G)/m-v(h)$. Suppose $g(v)/v(h)\\ge2c$ on some common lc ray, where $c>0$ is rational. Set $$M=\\max_{\\mathcal Q}g\\ge2c,\\qquad\n \\mu_*=(M-c)^{-1},\\qquad\n b_\\mu(v)=v(h)+\\mu(cv(h)-g(v)).$$ For $0\\le\\mu<\\mu_*$ the height is positive. There is a rational $\\mu$ in this interval whose $A=1$ minimizing valuation $w$ satisfies $$g(w)>0,\\qquad b_\\mu(w)\\ge1.$$ Moreover $b_\\mu\\le2+1/c$ on $\\mathcal Q$, and the corresponding central height obeys the same bound on $\\mathcal Q^0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G\\in R\\setminus\\{0\\}$, $m\\in\\mathbb Z_{>0}$, and $g(v)=v(G)/m-v(h)$. Suppose $g(v)/v(h)\\ge2c$ on some common lc ray, where $c>0$ is rational. There is a rational $\\mu$ in this interval whose $A=1$ minimizing valuation $w$ satisfies $$g(w)>0,\\qquad b_\\mu(w)\\ge1.$$ Moreover $b_\\mu\\le2+1/c$ on $\\mathcal Q$, and the corresponding central height obeys the same bound on $\\mathcal Q^0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-f-presentation",
        "exact_quote": "Choose $x_1,\\ldots,x_N\\in\\mathfrak m_x$ whose symbols generate $\\breve R$, and put $\\alpha_i=w(x_i)>0$. Any prescribed finite list of functions of positive $w$-value can be included in this list. Then $$\\label{eq:F-presentation}\n \\widehat{\\mathcal O}_{S,x}=\\mathbb C[[X_1,\\ldots,X_N]]/I,\n \\qquad\n \\breve R=\\mathbb C[X_1,\\ldots,X_N]/\\operatorname{in}_{\\alpha}I,$$ where initial forms have least weight. The valuation ideals are exactly the images of the ideals generated by monomials of weight at least the prescribed threshold. The same description holds before completion by intersection.*\n\n*For every sufficiently nearby linear functional $L:\\Lambda_w\\otimes\\mathbb R\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R$, positive on the listed weights, the vector $\\alpha_L=(L(\\alpha_i))$ gives the same initial ideal, with its grading composed with $L$. Its filtration defines a centred quasi-monomial valuation $w_L$. The functionals can be chosen rational, and the resulting valuations can be computed monomially on a fixed smooth chart for $w$.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-f-presentation",
          "exact_quote": "Choose $x_1,\\ldots,x_N\\in\\mathfrak m_x$ whose symbols generate $\\breve R$, and put $\\alpha_i=w(x_i)>0$. Then $$\\label{eq:F-presentation}\n \\widehat{\\mathcal O}_{S,x}=\\mathbb C[[X_1,\\ldots,X_N]]/I,\n \\qquad\n \\breve R=\\mathbb C[X_1,\\ldots,X_N]/\\operatorname{in}_{\\alpha}I,$$ where initial forms have least weight. The valuation ideals are exactly the images of the ideals generated by monomials of weight at least the prescribed threshold.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-f-canonical-degeneration",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses preceding Lemma <a href=\"#lem:F-presentation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:F-presentation\">41</a>, $S^0$ has a divisor-free reflexive canonical generator $\\omega^0$. It has the $G$-character of $\\omega$. It is homogeneous for $\\mathbb T_w$, and its weight $\\kappa$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:F-canonical-weight}\n \\langle w,\\kappa\\rangle=A_S(w).$$ If an additional torus acts on the original germ, preserves $w$, and makes $\\omega$ homogeneous, its character is preserved separately. None of these assertions assumes $A_S(w)=w(h)$. Moreover the action of $G$ on $S^0$ is quasi-étale.",

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-f-lc-degeneration",
        "exact_quote": "If $w$ is $h$-lc, then $(S^0,\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\bar h))$ is lc and $\\omega^0$ has the same fine torus weight and $G$-character as $\\bar h$. If $\\Delta$ is any effective rational sum of principal divisors near $x$, with $(S,\\Delta)$ lc and $A_S(w)=w(\\Delta)$, its initial boundary $\\Delta^0$ is lc on $S^0$.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-f-bridge",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $w$ is common $h$-lc and $w(f_j)=0$ for every $j$. Let $g=U/h^b$, where $0\\ne U\\in\\mathcal O_{S,x}$, $b\\in\\mathbb N_{>0}$, and $w(g)=0$. Then $$\\label{eq:F-bridge}\n q(g)\\le0\\quad(q\\in\\mathcal Q)\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n q^0(\\bar g)\\le0\\quad(q^0\\in\\mathcal Q^0).$$ One may add any finite list of tests of value zero at $w$.         "exact_quote": "Suppose $w$ is common $h$-lc. If every common actual valuation satisfies $q(f_j)=0$, then every common cone valuation satisfies $q^0(\\bar f_j)=0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-f-compression",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $w$ is as above, is $h$-lc, and satisfies $w(f_j)=0$ for every $j$. Let $v$ be a centred $G\\times\\mathbb T_w$-invariant, $\\bar h$-lc quasi-monomial valuation on $S^0$, with normal finitely generated klt graded algebra $R^{00}$. Suppose also $v(\\bar f_j)=0$. The algebra $R^{00}$ has its joint grading by the old torus and the $v$-grading. For arbitrarily small positive $\\delta$ outside a countable exceptional set, it is the associated graded of an actual centred quasi-monomial valuation $w_\\delta$ on $S$, whose grading evaluates a joint degree $(a,b)$ as $a+\\delta b$. The valuation $w_\\delta$ is $h$-lc, is exact on all $f_j$, and, after $h=1$ normalization, tends multiplicatively to $w$ uniformly on all nonzero regular functions. If $v$ is nonzero on an old degree-zero rational function, the rational rank of $w_\\delta$ is strictly larger than that of $w$.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },

The algebra $R^{00}$ has its joint grading by the old torus and the $v$-grading.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-f-refinement",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a sequence of the preceding data in dimensions at most $n_0$. Suppose that the common actual slices satisfy $q(f_j)=0$ identically, that $w$ is a centred point of the slice with the stipulated normal finitely generated klt graded algebra, and that the cones satisfy the following balance condition with one constant $C$ along the sequence: $$\\label{eq:F-balance}\n q^0(s)\\le Cw^0(s)\n \\quad\\begin{gathered}\n 0\\ne s\\in\\breve R\\text{ fine-homogeneous},\\\\[-2pt]\n q^0\\text{ common},\\quad q^0(\\bar h)=w(h)=1.\n \\end{gathered}$$ After passage to a subsequence, the $w$ can be replaced by $w'$ with all these properties, with a new fixed balance constant, and with $$\\label{eq:F-refinement-comparison}\n w'(f)=(1+o(1))w(f)\n \\qquad(0\\ne f\\in\\mathcal O_{S,x}),$$ uniformly in $f$ in the normalization $w'(h)=w(h)=1$. On the angular model $V_{\\mathbf u}$ of the new cone, the notation of Section <a href=\"#sec:D\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:D\">4</a> then satisfies $$\\label{eq:F-angular-order}\n T(\\Gamma)\\le(1+\\eta_i)D(T),\\qquad \\eta_i\\longrightarrow0,$$ for every effective $\\mathbb Q$-divisor $\\Gamma\\sim_\\mathbb QD$ and every primitive divisorial $T$ such that $a(T)=0$ and $T(\\bar f_j)\\ge0$ for all $j$. This is condition <span class=\"upright\">(C-$\\alpha$)</span> of Section <a href=\"#sec:C\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:C\">3</a>. The initial symbols here refer to $w'$.*\n\n*The balance hypothesis holds when $w$ is the exact minimizer on the common slice for the height $q(h)$.",

      },

On the angular model $V_{\\mathbf u}$ of the new cone, the notation of Section <a href=\"#sec:D\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:D\">4</a> then satisfies $$\\label{eq:F-angular-order}\n T(\\Gamma)\\le(1+\\eta_i)D(T),\\qquad \\eta_i\\longrightarrow0,$$ for every effective $\\mathbb Q$-divisor $\\Gamma\\sim_\\mathbb QD$ and every primitive divisorial $T$ such that $a(T)=0$ and $T(\\bar f_j)\\ge0$ for all $j$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-g-pinning",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $n>0$ and $\\epsilon>0$. There is no sequence of the following data satisfying all the conditions below. For each index $i$, let $x_i\\in S_i$ be an $n$-dimensional complex klt germ with an action of a finite cyclic group $\\Gamma_i$ fixing $x_i$, such that the quotient map is quasi-étale and $S_i/\\Gamma_i$ is $\\epsilon$-lc. Suppose $K_{S_i}$ has a nowhere-vanishing local eigen-generator $\\omega_i$, and let $h_i$ be a nonzero regular eigenfunction with the same character $\\chi_i$ as $\\omega_i$. The character need not be faithful. Assume that $(S_i,\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(h_i))$ is lc and admits a centred quasi-monomial valuation $T_i$ with $$A_{S_i}(T_i)=T_i(h_i)=1.$$ The excluded additional condition is: for every fixed integer $m>0$, there is an index $i(m)$ such that, for all $i\\ge i(m)$ and every regular semi-invariant $s$ of character $\\chi_i^m$, $$\\label{eq:G-input}\n                         T_i(s)\\ge m.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-g-pinning",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $n>0$ and $\\epsilon>0$. There is no sequence of the following data satisfying all the conditions below. Suppose $K_{S_i}$ has a nowhere-vanishing local eigen-generator $\\omega_i$, and let $h_i$ be a nonzero regular eigenfunction with the same character $\\chi_i$ as $\\omega_i$. The character need not be faithful. Assume that $(S_i,\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(h_i))$ is lc and admits a centred quasi-monomial valuation $T_i$ with $$A_{S_i}(T_i)=T_i(h_i)=1.$$ The excluded additional condition is: for every fixed integer $m>0$, there is an index $i(m)$ such that, for all $i\\ge i(m)$ and every regular semi-invariant $s$ of character $\\chi_i^m$, $$\\label{eq:G-input}\n                         T_i(s)\\ge m.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-g-noncancellation",
        "exact_quote": "At the preceding stage, consider any finite linear combination of monomials in the accumulated and newly added $Y_j^+$ and the current $G_a$, with nonnegative exponents. Suppose its least-$w$ group has a nonzero sum of initial symbols. If all its terms have the same value at an actual point of the pre-pinning slice, their sum has that value. This assertion holds eventually simultaneously in all polynomial degrees. Moreover, at a later working valuation on this slice where these terms are tied, the same non-cancellation assertion transfers to its cone at every common test where the terms remain tied and the pair values are opposite.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-g-noncancellation",
          "exact_quote": "At the preceding stage, consider any finite linear combination of monomials in the accumulated and newly added $Y_j^+$ and the current $G_a$, with nonnegative exponents. Suppose its least-$w$ group has a nonzero sum of initial symbols. If all its terms have the same value at an actual point of the pre-pinning slice, their sum has that value. This assertion holds eventually simultaneously in all polynomial degrees. Moreover, at a later working valuation on this slice where these terms are tied, the same non-cancellation assertion transfers to its cone at every common test where the terms remain tied and the pair values are opposite.",
          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "At the preceding stage, consider any finite linear combination of monomials in the accumulated and newly added $Y_j^+$ and the current $G_a$, with nonnegative exponents. Suppose its least-$w$ group has a nonzero sum of initial symbols. If all its terms have the same value at an actual point of the pre-pinning slice, their sum has that value. This assertion holds eventually simultaneously in all polynomial degrees. Moreover, at a later working valuation on this slice where these terms are tied, the same non-cancellation assertion transfers to its cone at every common test where the terms remain tied and the pair values are opposite.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-g-product-growth",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a finite-dimensional subspace of a function field over an algebraically closed field, containing $1$. Put $g=\\dim V$ and $a=\\mathop{\\mathrm{trdeg}}k(V)\\le n$. If $V^{(N)}$ is the span of $N$-fold products of members of $V$, there is $c_n>0$, depending only on $n$, such that $$\\dim V^{(N)}\\ge c_n gN^a\n                       \\qquad(N\\ge1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-g-product-growth",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a finite-dimensional subspace of a function field over an algebraically closed field, containing $1$. Put $g=\\dim V$ and $a=\\mathop{\\mathrm{trdeg}}k(V)\\le n$.           "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $V$ be a finite-dimensional subspace of a function field over an algebraically closed field, containing $1$. Put $g=\\dim V$ and $a=\\mathop{\\mathrm{trdeg}}k(V)\\le n$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-g-high-pin",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a high stage with pre-pinning slice, working valuation $w$, baseline height $\\mathfrak B$, scale $L$, and $s$ selected values as above. After passage to a subsequence, finitely many invariant tests of cost $O(L)$ can be added with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The resulting slice contains a centred valuation. The added tests are exact on that slice, all old tests and proportionalities remain in force, and the accumulated positive symbols remain algebraically independent at the exact minimizer and working valuation in <span class=\"upright\">(iii)</span> below.*\n\n2.  *The selected values either vanish or lie on a single rational line. In the line case their proportions have the bounds <a href=\"#eq:G-proportions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:G-proportions\">[eq:G-proportions]</a>, with $L_-=L$. An old anchor remains the anchor; if a new anchor is chosen, its scale is $L_1=L$.*\n\n3.  *The baseline height is retained unless an anchor is first chosen, in which case it may change to the rational height <a href=\"#eq:G-baseline\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:G-baseline\">[eq:G-baseline]</a>. It satisfies <a href=\"#eq:G-baseline-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:G-baseline-bounds\">[eq:G-baseline-bounds]</a> on the new actual slice. At its exact minimizer $v'$, and at an arbitrarily close rank refinement $w'$, the anchor, when present, satisfies $|v'(Y_0^+)|,|w'(Y_0^+)|\\ge cL_1$. Both valuations dominate a fixed positive multiple of $w$ on every regular function. In particular, $$w'\\ge c w.$$*\n\n*Consequently <a href=\"#it:G-tests\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"it:G-tests\">[it:G-tests]</a>–<a href=\"#it:G-working\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"it:G-working\">[it:G-working]</a> hold with $L_-=L$. The next-scale assertions <a href=\"#it:G-lattice\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"it:G-lattice\">[it:G-lattice]</a>–<a href=\"#it:G-field\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"it:G-field\">[it:G-field]</a> are not assumed in constructing the pin or its rank refinement.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-h-saturation",
        "exact_quote": "For each example there is a primitive divisorial order $w$ over $X$ whose image centre on $Z$ contains the specified point and for which $$\\label{eq:H-saturation}\n w(\\Delta_s)\\ge k A_X(w)\\quad\n       (k>0,\\ k\\mid M_i),\\qquad\n w(\\Delta_{s_b})=bA_X(w).$$ After replacing the example by a geometric generic slice, one may assume that the image centre is a closed point $z$, that $1\\le\\dim X=\\dim Z\\le d$, and that all the inequalities hold for the complete local systems at $z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-h-saturation",
          "exact_quote": "For each example there is a primitive divisorial order $w$ over $X$ whose image centre on $Z$ contains the specified point and for which $$\\label{eq:H-saturation}\n w(\\Delta_s)\\ge k A_X(w)\\quad\n       (k>0,\\ k\\mid M_i),\\qquad\n w(\\Delta_{s_b})=bA_X(w).$$ After replacing the example by a geometric generic slice, one may assume that the image centre is a closed point $z$, that $1\\le\\dim X=\\dim Z\\le d$, and that all the inequalities hold for the complete local systems at $z$.",
          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For each example there is a primitive divisorial order $w$ over $X$ whose image centre on $Z$ contains the specified point and for which $$\\label{eq:H-saturation}\n w(\\Delta_s)\\ge k A_X(w)\\quad\n       (k>0,\\ k\\mid M_i),\\qquad\n w(\\Delta_{s_b})=bA_X(w).$$ After replacing the example by a geometric generic slice, one may assume that the image centre is a closed point $z$, that $1\\le\\dim X=\\dim Z\\le d$, and that all the inequalities hold for the complete local systems at $z$.",
      "explanation": "A primitive divisorial order saturates the complete anti-canonical systems at the specified point.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-h-saturation",
          "exact_quote": "For each example there is a primitive divisorial order $w$ over $X$ whose image centre on $Z$ contains the specified point and for which $$\\label{eq:H-saturation}\n w(\\Delta_s)\\ge k A_X(w)\\quad\n       (k>0,\\ k\\mid M_i),\\qquad\n w(\\Delta_{s_b})=bA_X(w).$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-h-canonical-cover",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $f$ is an isomorphism near $z$. There is a normal integral cyclic cover $S_0\\to X$, with a unique point $o$ over $z$, a quasi-étale cyclic action fixing $o$, and a divisor-free canonical generator of character one. It has a regular eigenfunction $f_b=s_bt^b$ of character $b$ and a divisorial valuation $w_0$ centred at $o$ such that, with $A_0=A_{S_0}(w_0)>0$, $$\\label{eq:H-upstairs-saturation}\n (S_0,\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(f_b)/b)\\text{ is lc},\\qquad\n w_0(f_b)=bA_0,\\qquad\n w_0(a)\\ge kA_0$$ for every regular eigenfunction $a$ of character $k$ when $k>0$ and $k\\mid M_i$. The variety $S_0$ is klt.",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-h-canonical-cover",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $f$ is an isomorphism near $z$. It has a regular eigenfunction $f_b=s_bt^b$ of character $b$ and a divisorial valuation $w_0$ centred at $o$ such that, with $A_0=A_{S_0}(w_0)>0$, $$\\label{eq:H-upstairs-saturation}\n (S_0,\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(f_b)/b)\\text{ is lc},\\qquad\n w_0(f_b)=bA_0,\\qquad\n w_0(a)\\ge kA_0$$ for every regular eigenfunction $a$ of character $k$ when $k>0$ and $k\\mid M_i$. The variety $S_0$ is klt.",
          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose $f$ is an isomorphism near $z$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-h-signed-torsor",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $f$ is not an isomorphism near $z$. The signed algebra $$\\label{eq:H-signed-algebra}\n R=\\bigoplus_{k\\in\\mathbb Z}\n       H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(-kK_X))t^k,\\qquad S_0=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R,$$ over the affine base is a finitely generated normal domain of dimension $\\dim X+1$. Write $R_k=H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(-kK_X))t^k$ for its homogeneous part of degree $k$. Its *positive chamber* is the open $$S_0^+=\\bigcup_{k>0}\\ \\bigcup_{0\\ne a\\in R_k}D_{S_0}(a),$$ where $D_{S_0}(a)$ is the principal open on which $a$ does not vanish. This chamber has complement of codimension at least two. The form $$\\label{eq:H-torsor-form}\n \\Omega_0=t\\omega_X\\wedge d\\log t$$ is a divisor-free canonical generator of grading weight one, and every finite scalar grading subgroup acts quasi-étalely.*\n\n\n*The variety $S_0$ is klt, and $(S_0,\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(s_bt^b)/b)$ is lc. There is a grading-fixed closed point $o$ over $z$ and a rational Gauss valuation $w_0$ centred there which satisfies <a href=\"#eq:H-upstairs-saturation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:H-upstairs-saturation\">[eq:H-upstairs-saturation]</a> for every actual positive degree $k\\mid M_i$. Moreover there are numbers $\\alpha>0$ and $F_w>\\alpha$, depending on the example, for which every nonzero homogeneous section satisfies $$\\label{eq:H-two-slopes}\n w_0(s t^k)\\ge\n \\begin{cases}\n k\\alpha,&k>0,\\\\\n |k|(F_w-\\alpha),&k<0.\n \\end{cases}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-066.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-h-signed-torsor",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $f$ is not an isomorphism near $z$. Write $R_k=H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(-kK_X))t^k$ for its homogeneous part of degree $k$. Its *positive chamber* is the open $$S_0^+=\\bigcup_{k>0}\\ \\bigcup_{0\\ne a\\in R_k}D_{S_0}(a),$$ where $D_{S_0}(a)$ is the principal open on which $a$ does not vanish. This chamber has complement of codimension at least two. The form $$\\label{eq:H-torsor-form}\n \\Omega_0=t\\omega_X\\wedge d\\log t$$ is a divisor-free canonical generator of grading weight one, and every finite scalar grading subgroup acts quasi-étalely.*\n\n\n*The variety $S_0$ is klt, and $(S_0,\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(s_bt^b)/b)$ is lc. There is a grading-fixed closed point $o$ over $z$ and a rational Gauss valuation $w_0$ centred there which satisfies <a href=\"#eq:H-upstairs-saturation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:H-upstairs-saturation\">[eq:H-upstairs-saturation]</a> for every actual positive degree $k\\mid M_i$. Moreover there are numbers $\\alpha>0$ and $F_w>\\alpha$, depending on the example, for which every nonzero homogeneous section satisfies $$\\label{eq:H-two-slopes}\n w_0(s t^k)\\ge\n \\begin{cases}\n k\\alpha,&k>0,\\\\\n |k|(F_w-\\alpha),&k<0.\n \\end{cases}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-066.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose $f$ is not an isomorphism near $z$. The signed algebra $$\\label{eq:H-signed-algebra}\n R=\\bigoplus_{k\\in\\mathbb Z}\n       H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(-kK_X))t^k,\\qquad S_0=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R,$$ over the affine base is a finitely generated normal domain of dimension $\\dim X+1$. Write $R_k=H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X(-kK_X))t^k$ for its homogeneous part of degree $k$. Its *positive chamber* is the open $$S_0^+=\\bigcup_{k>0}\\ \\bigcup_{0\\ne a\\in R_k}D_{S_0}(a),$$ where $D_{S_0}(a)$ is the principal open on which $a$ does not vanish. This chamber has complement of codimension at least two. The form $$\\label{eq:H-torsor-form}\n \\Omega_0=t\\omega_X\\wedge d\\log t$$ is a divisor-free canonical generator of grading weight one, and every finite scalar grading subgroup acts quasi-étalely.*\n\n\n*The variety $S_0$ is klt, and $(S_0,\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(s_bt^b)/b)$ is lc. There is a grading-fixed closed point $o$ over $z$ and a rational Gauss valuation $w_0$ centred there which satisfies <a href=\"#eq:H-upstairs-saturation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:H-upstairs-saturation\">[eq:H-upstairs-saturation]</a> for every actual positive degree $k\\mid M_i$. Moreover there are numbers $\\alpha>0$ and $F_w>\\alpha$, depending on the example, for which every nonzero homogeneous section satisfies $$\\label{eq:H-two-slopes}\n w_0(s t^k)\\ge\n \\begin{cases}\n k\\alpha,&k>0,\\\\\n |k|(F_w-\\alpha),&k<0.\n \\end{cases}$$",
      "explanation": "In the non-isomorphic case the signed anti-canonical algebra gives a klt torsor with a two-slope saturated valuation.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **contraction** ([source](#sec:A)): A *contraction* is a projective surjective morphism of normal integral varieties with connected fibres, expressed by $f_*\mathcal O_X=\mathcal O_Z$.
- **Fano type** ([source](#sec:A)): It is of *Fano type* if an effective rational boundary $C$ exists such that $(X,C)$ is klt and $-(K_X+C)$ is ample over $Z$.
- **monotonic klt complement** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Over a neighbourhood of a base point, a *monotonic klt $n$-complement* of $(X,B)$ is an effective rational divisor $\Lambda\ge B$ such that $(X,\Lambda)$ is klt and $$n(K_X+\Lambda)\sim 0.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-comparison-tools** [claim](#stmt-lem-a-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-a-lattice-approximation-2): Induct on dimension through Mori steps using tailored complements and integral approximation. -> discrepancy comparison on the base
- **step-charts** [claim](#stmt-prop-a-uniform-charts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-a-toroidal-charts): Toroidalize bounded families and use Gauss restriction plus degree bounds. -> uniform Lipschitz charts
- **step-phase-nonvanishing** [claim](#stmt-lem-b-phase-nonvanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-b-principal-order-2): Dimension induction with Seifert bundles, corrections and MMP. -> bounded sections
- **step-saturation** [claim](#stmt-lem-c-saturation-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-c-saturation-count): Cutoff fields and section counts via jet bodies. -> neutral fields and counts
- **step-exact-minimizer** [claim](#stmt-thm-e-exact-minimizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-e-exact-minimizer): Volume interpolation and exact realization by a rational klt pair. -> unique minimizer with degeneration
- **step-pinning** [claim](#stmt-thm-g-pinning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-g-pin-consequences-2): Induction adding independent variables, bounded by dimension. -> pinning obstruction
- **step-birational-complements** [claim](#stmt-thm-birational) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-h-field-reduction-2): Contradiction: failure sequence gives pinning input. -> bounded klt complements
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-h-field-reduction-3): Apply Chen's equivalence and reduce thresholds to point ideals. -> uniform Cartier sections

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-uniform-cartier-threshold**: For fixed dimension and fixed discrepancy gap there is one positive threshold, depending only on these two parameters, such that at every closed point of the base a nonzero effective Cartier divisor through that point can be chosen with the pair obtained by adding the threshold times the pulled-back divisor log canonical over a neighbourhood of the point.
- **inv-birational-klt-complement-index**: For fixed dimension and discrepancy gap there is a single positive integer such that every epsilon-lc rationally Gorenstein birational Fano type contraction with nef negative canonical class admits, over a neighbourhood of any closed point, a klt complement of that index that is Cartier and linearly trivial.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-compute-threshold**: Use the main theorem to compute a numerical value of the uniform threshold or of the complement index for given dimension and discrepancy gap. -> The statement only asserts existence of the constants; no formula or procedure for them is available, so any computed value has no support.
- **anti-zero-dimensional-base**: Apply the Cartier-section theorem to a contraction onto a point. -> The theorem is stated only for a positive-dimensional base, so no conclusion about a divisor through a base point follows in the zero-dimensional case.
- **anti-positive-characteristic**: Transport the uniform threshold to varieties over a field of positive or mixed characteristic. -> The arguments and hypotheses are set only in characteristic zero, so the conclusion is unsupported elsewhere.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence statements for constants and divisors obtained by contradiction along sequences of counterexamples; no finite procedure computes them, and the source states that the constant is non-effective: 'It is non-effective; the divisor and the neighbourhood may depend on the contraction and the point.'

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-nonconstructive-constants** (nonconstructive): The uniform threshold and the complement index are produced by contradiction along infinite failure sequences. The source states that the constant is non-effective, so no value or formula for it exists. Blocked use: ['computing a numerical value of the Cartier threshold from the dimension and the discrepancy gap', 'computing a numerical complement index for a given pair', 'quoting a running time or an explicit neighbourhood for the divisor']
- **limit-positive-dimensional-base** (hypothesis): The Cartier-section theorem is stated for a base of positive dimension. No conclusion about a divisor through a base point is asserted when the base is a point. Blocked use: ['applying the Cartier-section theorem to a contraction onto a point', 'reading the theorem as covering the identity-contraction case without the reduction recalled in the source']
- **limit-nef-negative-log-class** (hypothesis): The conclusion needs the negative log canonical class to be nef over the base. Without that hypothesis the log canonicity statement is not claimed. Blocked use: ['using the threshold bound for contractions whose negative log canonical class is not nef over the base', 'dropping the nefness condition when passing to a different boundary']
- **limit-characteristic-zero** (formal_scope): All objects live over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Rational boundaries are treated in that setting and real boundaries only over the complex numbers. Blocked use: ['transporting the uniform threshold to positive or mixed characteristic', 'using the real-boundary corollary over a field other than the complex numbers']
- **limit-real-boundary-complex-only** (formal_scope): The real-boundary extension holds over the complex numbers and requires the log canonical class to be real Cartier. It keeps the same dependence on dimension and discrepancy gap. Blocked use: ['applying the corollary to real boundaries whose log canonical class is not real Cartier', 'claiming a different or better dependence of the constant on the dimension and the gap']
- **limit-birational-zero-boundary-input** (hypothesis): The complement theorem is for birational Fano type contractions of rationally Gorenstein varieties with nef negative canonical class. It is the zero-boundary birational input to the equivalence, not a statement for arbitrary boundaries. Blocked use: ['using the bounded index for pairs with a nonzero boundary directly', 'using it for non-birational contractions without the equivalence recalled in the source']
- **limit-proof-by-contradiction-sequences** (nonconstructive): The pinning argument terminates by a dimension count on independent variables after passing to subsequences. It excludes a hypothetical sequence and does not construct a pinning valuation for a given germ. Blocked use: ['extracting an explicit stage count or pinned valuation for a particular germ', 'treating subsequence extractions as a finite algorithm']
- **limit-point-ideal-threshold-loss** (approximation): A uniform lower bound for the threshold of the point ideal gives the Cartier-section statement only after replacing the bound by a smaller number below one. The constant is therefore not the ideal threshold itself. Blocked use: ['identifying the Cartier coefficient with the lct of the maximal ideal', 'reusing the ideal threshold bound as the coefficient without the stated reduction']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 066 (`lean/docs/066.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Uniform Cartier sections for Fano type contractions](../../preprints/Uniform-Cartier-sections-for-Fano-type-contractions-September-25-2026/Uniform-Cartier-sections-for-Fano-type-contractions-September-25-2026.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The paper develops uniform Cartier sections for Fano-type contractions as a step toward bounded klt complements. The linked formalization proves a local compactness result: for positive numbers $a_i$, the chart-field retractions indexed by nonnegative weights satisfying $\sum_i a_iw_i=1$ form a compact set in the pointwise topology on the fraction field.
>
> The chart is built over an algebraically closed field from the localization of a polynomial ring at its origin. Its local domain is Noetherian, essentially of finite type and formally unramified over that localization, and formally étale over the polynomial ring. Uniform Cartier sections, bounded klt complements, and the paper's real-boundary and companion conclusions are outside this supporting statement.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `isCompact_closedChartFieldRetraction_cone` | lemma |

Comparator source `CartierChartCompactnessSupport.lean` (1365 bytes, sha256 `05de6915fb577137d01be2fcaab5ebcc252d80f628ffb913bea4bef87b54ea64`), verbatim:

```lean
import OAI.AlgebraicGeometry.CartierSections.FieldTopology
import OAI.AlgebraicGeometry.CartierSections.FieldRetraction

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators NNReal ENNReal
namespace CartierSections
section ClosedChartFieldTopology
universe u
local instance polynomialOrigin_isMaximal_topology {σ k : Type u} [Fintype σ] [Field k] :
    (MvPolynomial.idealOfVars σ k).IsMaximal :=
  polynomialOrigin_isMaximal

variable {σ k R A K : Type u} [Fintype σ] [Field k] [IsAlgClosed k]
  [CommRing R] [CommRing A] [IsLocalRing R] [IsLocalRing A] [IsNoetherianRing A]
  [IsDomain A] [Field K] [Algebra A K] [IsFractionRing A K]
  [Algebra (MvPolynomial σ k) R] [Algebra (MvPolynomial σ k) A]
  [Algebra k A] [IsScalarTower k (MvPolynomial σ k) A]
  [Algebra R A] [IsScalarTower (MvPolynomial σ k) R A]
  [IsLocalization.AtPrime R (MvPolynomial.idealOfVars σ k)]
  [IsLocalHom (algebraMap R A)] [Algebra.FormallyUnramified R A]
  [Algebra.EssFiniteType R A] [Algebra.FormallyEtale (MvPolynomial σ k) A]

lemma isCompact_closedChartFieldRetraction_cone
    (a : σ → NNReal) (ha : ∀ i, 0 < a i) :
    IsCompact ((fun w : σ → NNReal => fun x : K =>
      closedChartFieldRetraction (σ := σ) (k := k) (R := R) (A := A) w x) ''
      normalizedWeights a) := by
  sorry

end ClosedChartFieldTopology
end CartierSections

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Cartier-divisor conjecture of Birkar and Shokurov for rational boundaries in characteristic zero and for real boundaries over ℂ. For an <i>ϵ</i>-lc Fano type contraction with positive-dimensional base and nef negative log canonical class, a Cartier divisor through any prescribed base point can be chosen with pullback log canonical threshold bounded below in terms of the dimension and <i>ϵ</i> alone.

<a id="sec:A"></a>
### Introduction

Through a prescribed point of a singular variety, can one choose a Cartier hypersurface whose log canonical threshold is bounded away from zero? For a Fano type contraction $f:X\to Z$, the corresponding question asks for a hypersurface on $Z$ whose pullback has controlled singularities along the entire fibre. The hypersurface may depend on the contraction and the point. The desired lower bound depends only on the dimension of $X$ and a positive lower bound for its log discrepancies.

We work over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. A *contraction* is a projective surjective morphism of normal integral varieties with connected fibres, expressed by $f_*\mathcal O_X=\mathcal O_Z$. It is of *Fano type* if an effective rational boundary $C$ exists such that $(X,C)$ is klt and $-(K_X+C)$ is ample over $Z$. This auxiliary boundary need not equal the boundary in the following theorem.

A rational pair $(X,B)$ has effective rational boundary $B$ and rationally Cartier log canonical divisor $K_X+B$. If $p:W\to X$ is a smooth birational model and $E$ is a prime divisor on $W$, our discrepancy convention is $$a(E;X,B)=1+\operatorname{coeff}_E\bigl(K_W-p^*(K_X+B)\bigr).$$ Thus $\epsilon$-lc means $a(E;X,B)\geq\epsilon$ for every such $E$; lc means that they are nonnegative; and klt means that they are strictly positive.

The obstruction is visible in a single ratio. If $D$ has local equation $g$ on the base, log canonicity after adding $t f^*D$ requires $$t\le\frac{a(E;X,B)}{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(f^*g)}$$ for every prime divisor $E$ over the relevant inverse image with positive denominator. A discrepancy bound alone does not control those orders. Even on a smooth curve, the divisor $m[z]$ has threshold $1/m$. The problem is therefore to choose an equation through $z$ for which all these inequalities hold with one uniform positive $t$.

<div class="samepage">

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="maintheorem">

**Theorem 1** (Uniform Cartier sections). *For every integer $d>0$ and every real number $\epsilon>0$, there is a real number $\tau=\tau(d,\epsilon)>0$ with the following property. Let $(X,B)$ be an $\epsilon$-lc rational pair of dimension $d$, and let $f:X\to Z$ be a Fano type contraction of normal integral quasi-projective varieties with $\dim Z>0$. Suppose that $-(K_X+B)$ is nef over $Z$. For every closed point $z\in Z$, there are an open neighbourhood $U$ of $z$ and a nonzero effective Cartier divisor $D$ on $U$, with $z\in\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}D$, such that $$\bigl(f^{-1}(U),B|_{f^{-1}(U)}+\tau f^*D\bigr)
\quad\text{is log canonical.}$$*

</div>

</div>

The conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> concerns the whole inverse image of a neighbourhood of $z$. The constant is independent of Cartier indices, boundary denominators, and any choice of polarization. It is non-effective; the divisor and the neighbourhood may depend on the contraction and the point.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### Cartier sections and complements.

For the identity contraction, the question above concerns arbitrary $\epsilon$-lc singularities. Equivalently, it asks for a uniform positive lower bound for the log canonical threshold of the maximal ideal at a closed point; the passage to a general element of that ideal is recalled in Lemma <a href="#lem:H-field-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:H-field-reduction">59</a>. The general contraction requires the corresponding bound for the pulled-back point ideal. This is a different local question from bounding the discriminant coefficients in the canonical bundle formula: those coefficients are defined by thresholds over generic points of divisors on birational models of the base. A Cartier section through a prescribed point must control the whole fibre at once.

The complement formulation asks for a uniformly bounded complement index. Over a neighbourhood of a base point, a *monotonic klt $n$-complement* of $(X,B)$ is an effective rational divisor $\Lambda\ge B$ such that $(X,\Lambda)$ is klt and $$n(K_X+\Lambda)\sim 0.$$ Here linear equivalence includes the assertion that the displayed divisor is Cartier. In Shokurov’s boundedness conjecture, as formulated in (Chen 2025, Conjecture 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Chen2025](#ref-Chen2025)], the index $n$ depends only on the total dimension $d$, the discrepancy bound $\epsilon>0$, and a fixed finite set $\mathfrak R\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$ containing the coefficients of $B$. The geometric hypotheses are those of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, with the zero-dimensional base also allowed.

Complements entered birational geometry through Shokurov’s work on threefold log flips, including bounded local lc complements for surfaces (V. V. Shokurov 1993, sec. 5) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[ShokurovFlips](#ref-ShokurovFlips)]. His surface theorem and bounded-complement conjectures developed the relation between mild singularities and bounded complement indices (V. V. Shokurov 2000, Conjecture 1.3 and Main Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[ShokurovSurfaces](#ref-ShokurovSurfaces)]. The stronger expectation that bounded complements preserve a positive lower bound on log discrepancies appears in his 2004 formulation (V. Shokurov 2004) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ShokurovBounded2004](#ref-ShokurovBounded2004)]. Birkar proved boundedness of lc complements in arbitrary dimension for Fano type pairs with hyperstandard coefficients (Birkar 2019, Theorems 1.7–1.8) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BirkarComplements](#ref-BirkarComplements)]. Those lc complements provide the bounded initial index used below. A klt complement must have strictly positive discrepancies, and obtaining this additional property uniformly is the issue addressed here.

Several earlier results describe the reach of the Cartier-section problem. Sankaran and Santos bounded the smallest weight of an $\epsilon$-lc weighted blowup of affine space (Sankaran and Santos 2021, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[SankaranSantos](#ref-SankaranSantos)], proving the weighted-blowup case of Birkar’s question. For torus-invariant boundaries with coefficients in a fixed DCC set (one with no infinite strictly decreasing sequence), Ambro constructed invariant Cartier sections through torus-fixed base points in the toric setting (Ambro 2025, Theorem 3.12, arXiv version 2) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[AmbroToric](#ref-AmbroToric)]. Under his hyperstandard coefficient hypothesis, his Theorem 1.2 also gives bounded complements preserving a positive discrepancy bound. Birkar’s control of base singularities gives the Cartier assertion over curves, together with bounded klt complements in the curve-base case (Birkar 2023b, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BirkarCY](#ref-BirkarCY)]. These curve and toric results illustrate two distinct ways to control the orders appearing in the threshold inequalities.

Over $\mathbb C$, Chen proves the decisive general reduction. For each bound $\ell$ on the base dimension, bounded klt complements, the Cartier-section assertion, and its identity-contraction case are equivalent; on the complement side, birational contractions with zero boundary suffice (Chen 2025, Theorem 1.6) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Chen2025](#ref-Chen2025)]. He proves the surface local assertion with coefficient $\epsilon^2/24$, and hence both conjectures for bases of dimension at most two (Chen 2025, Theorem 1.7 and Corollary 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Chen2025](#ref-Chen2025)]. He also removes the coefficient restriction in the toric Cartier-section assertion (Chen 2025, Theorem 1.11) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Chen2025](#ref-Chen2025)]. We use the precise local formulation of the Birkar–Shokurov conjecture in (Chen 2025, Conjecture 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Chen2025](#ref-Chen2025)]; the relatively ample formulation also appears in (Birkar 2025, Conjecture 1.27) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BirkarSurvey](#ref-BirkarSurvey)].

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> proves the rational-boundary assertion in characteristic zero, and Corollary <a href="#cor:real-boundaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:real-boundaries">3</a> below gives the real-boundary formulation over $\mathbb C$. The conjecture is therefore resolved positively in these settings. The new input to Chen’s reduction is the following birational theorem.

<a id="stmt-thm-birational"></a>
<div id="thm:birational" class="maintheorem">

**Theorem 2** (Birational klt complements). *For each $d>0$ and $\epsilon>0$, there is a positive integer $n=n(d,\epsilon)$ such that the following holds. Let $X$ be an $\epsilon$-lc rationally Gorenstein variety of dimension $d$, and let $f:X\to Z$ be a birational Fano type contraction with $-K_X$ nef over $Z$. For every closed $z\in Z$, there is an effective rational divisor $\Delta$ over a neighbourhood of $z$ such that $(X,\Delta)$ is klt there and $n(K_X+\Delta)$ is Cartier and linearly trivial there, after shrinking the neighbourhood.*

</div>

The companion article (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[OpenAIComplements](#ref-OpenAIComplements)] treats bounded klt complements for Fano contractions and their finite-rational-coefficient extension by an independent argument. The present proof establishes Theorem <a href="#thm:birational" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:birational">2</a> directly, through its own character and pinning obstruction.

Applying Chen’s Theorem 1.6 in arXiv version 4, implication (2)$\Rightarrow$(3), transfers Theorem <a href="#thm:birational" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:birational">2</a> to Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. For a fixed total dimension $d$, use the base-dimension bound $\ell=d$ and the birational theorem in every dimension at most $d$, for every positive discrepancy gap. Implication (2)$\Rightarrow$(1) of the same theorem also supplies the finite-rational-coefficient complement assertion stated above over $\mathbb C$. Its Cartier-section conclusion already allows real boundaries, giving the following extension.

<a id="stmt-cor-real-boundaries"></a>
<div id="cor:real-boundaries" class="maincorollary">

**Corollary 3** (Real boundaries). *Over $\mathbb C$, the conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> holds for effective real boundaries $B$ with $K_X+B$ real Cartier, with the same uniform dependence on $d$ and $\epsilon$.*

</div>

The reduction requires rationalization only in the zero-boundary birational case: an integral Weil divisor that is real Cartier is rationally Cartier, and a real klt log Fano witness can be chosen rational. Section <a href="#sec:H" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:H">8</a> supplies these details, the reduction from arbitrary algebraically closed characteristic-zero fields to $\mathbb C$ for Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, and the passage between point-ideal thresholds and individual Cartier divisors. No approximation of the arbitrary nef real boundary in Corollary <a href="#cor:real-boundaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:real-boundaries">3</a> is required.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### The argument.

Suppose that Theorem <a href="#thm:birational" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:birational">2</a> fails. Section <a href="#sec:H" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:H">8</a> uses bounded lc complements to construct a sequence of complex klt germs $x_i\in S_i$ of bounded dimension, with finite cyclic groups $\Gamma_i$ fixing $x_i$. The quotient maps $$S_i\longrightarrow S_i/\Gamma_i$$ are quasi-étale, meaning étale outside subsets of codimension at least two, and the quotient germs have a fixed positive discrepancy gap for primitive prime orders. On $S_i$, a nonzero regular function $h_i$ and a nowhere-vanishing canonical generator have the same character $\chi_i$. The pair $(S_i,\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(h_i))$ is lc, and a centred quasi-monomial valuation $T_i$ satisfies $$A_{S_i}(T_i)=T_i(h_i)=1,$$ where $A_{S_i}$ is the log-discrepancy function with zero boundary. Failure of bounded klt complements gives the stronger condition $$\forall m\in\mathbb Z_{>0}\ \exists i(m)\ \forall i\ge i(m):\qquad
 T_i(s)\ge m
 \quad\text{for every nonzero regular $s$ of character $\chi_i^m$.}$$ Thus the order bound holds for every section in each fixed character degree, rather than for a selected sequence of sections. The reduction uses a canonical cover in the isomorphism case and a signed anti-canonical grading for other birational contractions.

Character relations become concrete after division by powers of $h_i$.
